name: unslop
description: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.
---

# Unslop

Edit text to remove AI patterns and add human voice.

## Process

1. Scan for the patterns below.
2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.
3. Add soul (see next section).
4. Self-audit: "What makes this obviously AI generated?" Fix remaining tells.

## Adding soul

Removing patterns is half the job. Sterile, voiceless writing is just as obvious.

- **Have opinions.** React to facts instead of neutrally listing pros and cons.
- **Vary rhythm.** Short sentences. Then longer ones that take their time. Mix it up.
- **Acknowledge complexity.** "Impressive but also kind of unsettling" beats "impressive."
- **Use "I" when it fits.** First person isn't unprofessional.
- **Let some mess in.** Perfect structure looks machine-made.
- **Be specific.** Not "this is concerning" but "there's something unsettling about agents churning away at 3am."

## Patterns to detect and fix

### Content

1. **Puffery.** "pivotal moment", "testament to", "evolving landscape", "setting the stage for", "indelible mark", "deeply rooted". Cut puffery, state what happened.
2. **Name-dropping.** Listing media outlets without context. Pick one, say what was said.
3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** "highlighting...", "ensuring...", "reflecting...", "showcasing...", "fostering...". Delete or expand with real sources.
4. **Promotional language.** "nestled", "vibrant", "breathtaking", "groundbreaking", "renowned", "stunning", "must-visit". Use neutral descriptions.
5. **Vague attributions.** "Experts believe", "Industry reports suggest", "Some critics argue". Name the source or delete.
6. **Formulaic challenges.** "Despite challenges... continues to thrive." Replace with specific facts.

### Language

7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.
8. **Fancy ways to say "is".** "serves as", "stands as", "boasts", "features". Just say "is" or "has".
9. **Scripted negation.** "Not just X, but Y", "No X, no Y, no Z", "Didn't flinch, didn't blink", "Don't call it X, call it Y". State the claim directly once.
10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.
11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.
12. **False ranges.** "from X to Y" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.

### Style

13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). Em dashes are an AI tell, and reaching for parentheses instead just trades one tell for another. If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.
14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. "If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. "Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English." Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.
15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.
16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: "**Performance:** Performance improved...". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and adds new detail ("**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.") is fine, not a tell.
17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.
18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.
19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.

### Communication artifacts

20. **Chatbot phrases.** "I hope this helps", "Let me know if", "Would you like", "Of course", "Certainly", "Found the smoking gun", "One honest caveat", "Here's what gets me", "The key insight is", "The punchline is", "Sit with that", "You already know the answer". Delete the greeting, stage direction, or recap. State the point.
21. **Cutoff disclaimers.** "While specific details are limited..." Find sources or remove.
22. **Sycophantic tone.** "Great question! You're absolutely right!" Respond directly.
23. **Manufactured gravity.** "the real problem", "genuinely", "actually", "truly", "that's the whole point", "X is the entire game", "the improvement is real and not subtle", "that loss is worth naming", "that's not nothing". These insist something matters instead of showing why. Give the fact, consequence, or measurement. Keep an intensifier only when it marks a literal contrast.
24. **Anthropomorphized tools.** Code does not "want to live" somewhere, feel "happiest" there, or "earn its keep". Name the constraint: cost, speed, ownership, or repeatability.

### Filler

25. **Filler phrases.** "In order to" becomes "To". "Due to the fact that" becomes "Because". "It is important to note that" gets deleted.
26. **Excessive hedging.** "could potentially possibly be argued that it might" becomes "may".
27. **Generic conclusions.** "The future looks bright." State specific plans or facts.

### Jargon

28. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in "API surface"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. "Substrate" becomes "base". "Wedge in" becomes "add". "Vector" becomes "way" or "method". "Gold-plating" becomes "more than the job needs". "Ratchet" becomes the mechanism's real name or "a limit that only tightens". "Evacuate" becomes "move out". "Endgame" becomes "the last phase". Pick the concrete word.

### Plain speech

29. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
30. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
31. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or doesn't matter.
32. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number. "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
33. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" becomes "use", "leverage" becomes "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.

## Conventions (how we build)
- Open [**Talk only to the configured server and use only free software**](nodes/practice-talk-only-to-configured-server.md) to learn about: No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, ads or proprietary libraries. The app sends requests only to the configured server. #privacy #network #fdroid #security
- Open [**Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP**](nodes/practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http.md) to learn about: Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning. #security #network
- Open [**Run instrumented tests only through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh**](nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md) to learn about: The script starts a pinned Tokkl container, seeds an account and passes it to the tests. Plain connectedDebugAndroidTest fails them. #testing #android #emulator #docker
- Open [**Test against a real Tokkl server and catch speed regressions in CI**](nodes/practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions.md) to learn about: Instrumented tests use the real ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl image, never a fake. Macrobenchmark fails a build slower than the previous one. #testing #ci #performance
- Open [**Test on the plain AOSP API 36 emulator, headless, and stop it afterwards**](nodes/practice-emulator-plain-aosp-image-headless.md) to learn about: AVD tokkl-api36 uses the AOSP image, since ATD images drop SystemUI and the launcher. Boot it with -no-window -accel on and kill it after. #testing #android #emulator #energy
- Open [**Build with --no-daemon in parallel worktrees and never run --stop**](nodes/practice-gradle-no-daemon-in-parallel-worktrees.md) to learn about: ./gradlew --stop ends every daemon of that Gradle version for the user, including one serving a build in another worktree. #build #gradle #agents #worktrees
- Open [**Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone**](nodes/practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone.md) to learn about: Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account timezone read from GET /api/v9/me. #api #ui #time
- Open [**Port Tokkl's TypeScript with JavaScript semantics and verbatim test cases**](nodes/practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics.md) to learn about: The Kotlin time parsers copy JavaScript's whitespace, rounding and replace rules, return null for NaN, and reuse the .test.ts cases verbatim. #time #parsing #testing #tokkl
- Open [**Run npm install in every new clone or worktree to turn on the commit hook**](nodes/practice-run-npm-install-in-every-clone-and-worktree.md) to learn about: The commit-msg hook lives in git-ignored .husky/_, so a clone or worktree that skipped npm install commits with no checks. #git #commits #tooling
- Open [**Use Tokkl's palette, WCAG AA contrast and English string resources**](nodes/practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings.md) to learn about: Always Tokkl's palette, with wallpaper dynamic colour only as an opt-in. WCAG AA contrast, colour never the only signal, text in resources. #ui #accessibility #i18n
- Open [**Check server capabilities instead of requiring a server version**](nodes/practice-check-capabilities-not-server-version.md) to learn about: No minimum Tokkl version. A missing /me field or a 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint hides the features that depend on it. #api #toggl #compatibility
- Open [**Keep release builds reproducible and signed with the project key**](nodes/practice-reproducible-builds-project-key.md) to learn about: Reproducible builds let F-Droid publish the APK signed with the project key. The key lives in Actions secrets and CI pins actions by SHA. #release #fdroid #security #ci
- Open [**Never make Start or Stop wait for the server**](nodes/practice-never-wait-for-server-on-start-stop.md) to learn about: Taps change state on the phone at once and queue the change. The server is the source of truth, with a cache and queue, no sync engine. #offline #network #api
- Open [**Opt the Android SDK tools out of usage metrics with ~/.androidrc**](nodes/practice-opt-sdk-tools-out-of-usage-metrics.md) to learn about: sdkmanager 23.0 runs Google's android CLI, which sends usage data unless given --no-metrics. A ~/.androidrc holding --no-metrics opts out. #privacy #tooling #android
- Open [**Refresh on events, never on a short polling timer**](nodes/practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling.md) to learn about: Refresh when the app opens, after a change and when the network returns. No short polling, so Toggl's base URL later fits its quotas. #network #toggl #battery
- Open [**Version the app on its own, starting from a hand-made v0.1.0 tag**](nodes/practice-own-version-from-v0-1-0-tag.md) to learn about: The app has its own v* tags and changelog, independent of Tokkl. The first release is a hand-made v0.1.0 tag; semantic-release counts on. #release #versioning

# Tokkl for Android

Native Android client for the Tokkl server and for Toggl Track. The repo holds a Gradle project with a sign-in screen, a placeholder home screen, a settings screen and a Licences screen so far, joined by a navigation host; [docs/PRD.md](docs/PRD.md) is the source of truth for scope and decisions.

- The server lives in the sibling repo `../tokkl` (github.com/e0ipso/tokkl). Its `docs/PRD.md` section 6 lists the Toggl API v9 and Reports v3 endpoints Tokkl implements, and section 4 has the visual direction this app borrows from.
- Commits follow Conventional Commits, as in tokkl. Ticket IDs (`TKA-NNNN`, tracked with sift in `.ai/sift`) go in a `Refs:` trailer, never in the subject. Commits carry no AI or co-author credit lines.
[credential-related line omitted]

# kenkeep

> kenkeep navigation: the injected body above is the root index node, the top-level catalog of branches and root-level leaves. Do not expect the whole knowledge base here; descend on demand. Read the root index node, pick one or more branches whose intent and tags match your task (several branches can be relevant), and read those branch `index.md` nodes. Descend further only where the task needs it, opening only the leaves you have confirmed are relevant. Follow each leaf's `relates_to` and `depends_on` cross edges to reach related leaves in other branches. You decide how deep to go per branch.

## Branches
_None._

## Conventions (how we build)
- Open [**Talk only to the configured server and use only free software**](nodes/practice-talk-only-to-configured-server.md) to learn about: No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, ads or proprietary libraries. The app sends requests only to the configured server. #privacy #network #fdroid #security
- Open [**Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP**](nodes/practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http.md) to learn about: Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning. #security #network
- Open [**Run instrumented tests only through scripts/instrumented-tests.sh**](nodes/practice-run-instrumented-tests-only-through-the-script.md) to learn about: The script starts a pinned Tokkl container, seeds an account and passes it to the tests. Plain connectedDebugAndroidTest fails them. #testing #android #emulator #docker
- Open [**Test against a real Tokkl server and catch speed regressions in CI**](nodes/practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions.md) to learn about: Instrumented tests use the real ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl image, never a fake. Macrobenchmark fails a build slower than the previous one. #testing #ci #performance
- Open [**Test on the plain AOSP API 36 emulator, headless, and stop it afterwards**](nodes/practice-emulator-plain-aosp-image-headless.md) to learn about: AVD tokkl-api36 uses the AOSP image, since ATD images drop SystemUI and the launcher. Boot it with -no-window -accel on and kill it after. #testing #android #emulator #energy
- Open [**Build with --no-daemon in parallel worktrees and never run --stop**](nodes/practice-gradle-no-daemon-in-parallel-worktrees.md) to learn about: ./gradlew --stop ends every daemon of that Gradle version for the user, including one serving a build in another worktree. #build #gradle #agents #worktrees
- Open [**Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone**](nodes/practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone.md) to learn about: Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account timezone read from GET /api/v9/me. #api #ui #time
- Open [**Port Tokkl's TypeScript with JavaScript semantics and verbatim test cases**](nodes/practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics.md) to learn about: The Kotlin time parsers copy JavaScript's whitespace, rounding and replace rules, return null for NaN, and reuse the .test.ts cases verbatim. #time #parsing #testing #tokkl
- Open [**Run npm install in every new clone or worktree to turn on the commit hook**](nodes/practice-run-npm-install-in-every-clone-and-worktree.md) to learn about: The commit-msg hook lives in git-ignored .husky/_, so a clone or worktree that skipped npm install commits with no checks. #git #commits #tooling
- Open [**Use Tokkl's palette, WCAG AA contrast and English string resources**](nodes/practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings.md) to learn about: Always Tokkl's palette, with wallpaper dynamic colour only as an opt-in. WCAG AA contrast, colour never the only signal, text in resources. #ui #accessibility #i18n
- Open [**Check server capabilities instead of requiring a server version**](nodes/practice-check-capabilities-not-server-version.md) to learn about: No minimum Tokkl version. A missing /me field or a 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint hides the features that depend on it. #api #toggl #compatibility
- Open [**Keep release builds reproducible and signed with the project key**](nodes/practice-reproducible-builds-project-key.md) to learn about: Reproducible builds let F-Droid publish the APK signed with the project key. The key lives in Actions secrets and CI pins actions by SHA. #release #fdroid #security #ci
- Open [**Never make Start or Stop wait for the server**](nodes/practice-never-wait-for-server-on-start-stop.md) to learn about: Taps change state on the phone at once and queue the change. The server is the source of truth, with a cache and queue, no sync engine. #offline #network #api
- Open [**Opt the Android SDK tools out of usage metrics with ~/.androidrc**](nodes/practice-opt-sdk-tools-out-of-usage-metrics.md) to learn about: sdkmanager 23.0 runs Google's android CLI, which sends usage data unless given --no-metrics. A ~/.androidrc holding --no-metrics opts out. #privacy #tooling #android
- Open [**Refresh on events, never on a short polling timer**](nodes/practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling.md) to learn about: Refresh when the app opens, after a change and when the network returns. No short polling, so Toggl's base URL later fits its quotas. #network #toggl #battery
- Open [**Version the app on its own, starting from a hand-made v0.1.0 tag**](nodes/practice-own-version-from-v0-1-0-tag.md) to learn about: The app has its own v* tags and changelog, independent of Tokkl. The first release is a hand-made v0.1.0 tag; semantic-release counts on. #release #versioning

## Components (what exists)
- Open [**JDK and Android SDK on this machine, and how agent shells reach them**](nodes/map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells.md) to learn about: Temurin 21 in ~/.local/share/jdk/21, SDK in ~/Android/Sdk. Agents reach java, adb and the SDK tools through wrappers in ~/.local/bin. #android #tooling #agents #build
- Open [**API client: one OkHttpClient, ServerUrl, TogglApiClient and ApiError**](nodes/map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors.md) to learn about: The api package holds the app's only OkHttpClient, base URL handling, JSON calls, sendNoContent for empty bodies and typed errors. #api #network #okhttp
- Open [**F-Droid metadata layout and release artifacts**](nodes/map-fdroid-metadata-release-artifacts.md) to learn about: Store text and images live under fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/. F-Droid finds versions by git tag and the signed APK via Binaries. #fdroid #release
- Open [**Planned stack: Compose, Glance, TileService, Room, WorkManager, OkHttp**](nodes/map-planned-android-stack.md) to learn about: Proposed stack: Kotlin and Compose Material 3, Glance widget, TileService tile, Room cache and queue, WorkManager, OkHttp. #architecture #android #offline
- Open [**Tokkl and Toggl API endpoints, auth and Tokkl-only extensions**](nodes/map-tokkl-toggl-api.md) to learn about: Tokkl speaks a subset of Toggl API v9 at /api/v9 and Reports v3 at /reports/api/v3 with Basic auth, plus Tokkl-only fields and endpoints. #api #toggl #network
[credential-related line omitted]
- Open [**Application ID, license and supported Android versions**](nodes/map-app-id-license-android-versions.md) to learn about: Application ID com.github.e0ipso.tokkl, MIT license, Android 8.0 (API 26) and later, one server and one account at a time. #release #fdroid #android
- Open [**Gradle build settings: SDK levels, Kotlin version source and memory**](nodes/map-gradle-build-sdk-levels-kotlin-and-memory.md) to learn about: compileSdk follows the dependencies, targetSdk matches the emulator, the catalog sets Kotlin, and the Gradle heap stays small. #android #build #gradle
- Open [**T3 restarts daily and ends running agent sessions**](nodes/map-t3-daily-restart-stops-agent-sessions.md) to learn about: t3-update.timer fires daily with up to 1h delay and restarts t3.service even without an update, which stops every agent session. #agents #tooling #machine
- Open [**Tokkl colour tokens and Anybody font in ../tokkl**](nodes/map-tokkl-colour-tokens-and-font.md) to learn about: The colour tokens in ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css are the palette's source of truth. Tokkl ships Anybody under the SIL OFL 1.1. #ui #tokkl

title: Talk only to the configured server and use only free software
description: >-

  app sends requests only to the configured server.

Data stays between the phone and the server. The app has no telemetry, analytics, crash reporting or ads, and sends no request to any host other than the configured server. This carries Tokkl's first principle over and matches F-Droid's rules.

Every dependency must be free software. F-Droid builds the app from source with a free toolchain and rejects proprietary libraries such as Google Play Services and Firebase. So the app has no push through FCM.

`:app:checkDeniedDependencies` enforces this. It fails when the release runtime classpath holds a module from a group listed in `gradle/denied-dependency-groups.txt`, or from a group below one, and prints the path that pulled it in. Each line of that file reads `<group> # <reason>`. `check` depends on the task, and CI runs `./gradlew assembleDebug check`.

[opaque value omitted]
---
type: practice
title: Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone
description: >-
  Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account
  timezone read from GET /api/v9/me.
tags:
  - api
  - ui
  - time
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app uses the same time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo as Tokkl's web app, in the account's timezone. Moving between the laptop and the phone then needs no relearning.

Read the timezone, first day of the week and `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me`, and use the account timezone everywhere (S7). Show stopped entries as the server returns them, after it snaps them to `time_resolution_minutes` (T3). The phone snaps entries it has not sent yet with a port of `snapSpan` from `../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts`, in the account timezone, so their times do not change when the server answers.

Manual entry accepts times like `9`, `930`, `9:30` and `4pm`, entries across midnight, and durations like `1h 30m`, `90` and `1,5h` (M1). The parsing is a Kotlin port of Tokkl's `parseDuration` in `../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts` and `parseTimeOfDay` in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts`, checked against the cases in the `.test.ts` file next to each. The time-of-day parser lives in the web package, not in `packages/common`. Both ports live in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time`, and their tests copy the TypeScript cases verbatim at a recorded tokkl commit (see the porting practice node). The ports can still drift when the TypeScript changes later. Sharing the test cases between the two repos would catch that.

Favorites are the same ones the web app shows, read from and written to `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

[opaque value omitted]
---
type: practice
title: Port Tokkl's TypeScript with JavaScript semantics and verbatim test cases
description: >-
  The Kotlin time parsers copy JavaScript's whitespace, rounding and replace
  rules, return null for NaN, and reuse the .test.ts cases verbatim.
tags:
  - time
  - parsing
  - testing
  - tokkl
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The time parsers in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time` (`Duration.kt` for `parseDuration`, `TimeOfDay.kt` for `parseTimeOfDay`) behave like the TypeScript they port, including where Kotlin differs from JavaScript. `JsSemantics.kt` has the helpers. JavaScript's `\s` and `String.prototype.trim` use the ECMAScript WhiteSpace and LineTerminator characters, while Java's `\s` matches only ASCII and Kotlin's `trim()` uses yet another set. `Math.round` rounds halves up and `kotlin.math.round` rounds them to even. JavaScript's `replace(',', '.')` changes only the first comma, so the port calls `replaceFirst`.

A Long cannot hold NaN, Infinity or integers from 2^63 up, so where the TypeScript returns one of those the port returns null. `parseDuration` returns NaN for the unit `constructor`, because its unit lookup finds that key on `Object.prototype`, and Infinity once a number of 300-odd digits overflows a double.

`DurationTest` and `TimeOfDayTest` hold the cases of the matching `.test.ts` file verbatim, and their header names the tokkl commit they were copied at. To follow an upstream change, copy the cases again and update that commit. `DurationJsSemanticsTest` pins the JavaScript traps in its own file, since they are not in `duration.test.ts` and a later diff against tokkl leaves them alone. Its expected values come from running the TypeScript file in Node 24, where `node file.mts` strips the types.

The HTTP code lives in `app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/`. `SharedHttpClient.instance` is the app's only `OkHttpClient`, so its connection pool, dispatcher and security settings apply to every request. New security settings, such as the trust manager for a pinned self-signed certificate and the interceptor that blocks hosts other than the configured server, go into its `build()`, never into a second client.

`ServerUrl.parse` accepts an http or https base URL that may carry a path, such as `https://example.com/t

[credential-related line omitted]

`TogglApiClient.get` and `send` decode a 2xx body with a kotlinx.serialization deserializer, and an empty body fails to decode. Calls that expect no content use `sendNoContent`, which treats any 2xx as success and ignores the body. Tokkl's DELETE routes for time entries, tasks, clients and tags answer 200 with an empty body, while the project DELETE answers 200 with the project id as JSON (`../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/togglV9.ts`). `sendNoContent` handles both.

Every call returns `ApiResult.Success` or `ApiResult.Failure(ApiError)`. 403 maps to `AuthFailed`, 429 to `RateLimited` with the `Retry-After` seconds (null when the header is missing or not a whole number), and any other status outside 2xx to `UnexpectedStatus`. `Network` wraps the `IOException` of a request that got no response. `InvalidResponse` is a 2xx body that is not the expected JSON.

title: Keep release builds reproducible and signed with the project key
description: >-

If the build is reproducible, F-Droid publishes the APK signed with the project's own key, and only when its own build matches. F-Droid's docs ask for pinned build-tools and no embedded timestamps. Shipping builds signed with the project's own key also keeps registration for Google's developer verification possible later.

The signing key lives in GitHub Actions secrets, never in the repo.

CI on GitHub copies Tokkl's setup. One `ci.yml` workflow runs on pull requests and pushes to `main`. Every action is pinned to a commit SHA with its version in a comment, and Dependabot updates them. Jobs cover the Gradle build, lint and unit tests, and instrumented tests on an emulator. GitHub's Ubuntu runners need a udev rule before the emulator can use KVM. Today `ci.yml` has two jobs, `gradle` (`./gradlew assembleDebug check`) and `commitlint`. The emulator job and the Dependabot config do not exist yet.

---
type: practice
title: 'Use Tokkl''s palette, WCAG AA contrast and English string resources'
description: >-
  Always Tokkl's palette, with wallpaper dynamic colour only as an opt-in. WCAG
  AA contrast, colour never the only signal, text in resources.
tags:
  - ui
  - accessibility
  - i18n
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: medium
---
Components come from Compose Material 3. Colour is always Tokkl's palette, never the wallpaper. Dynamic color is off and exists at most as an opt-in setting (G2). The theme is light, dark, or follows the system (G1).

Anybody, bundled with the app, sets the running clock, totals and headings. Everything else uses the system font.

Contrast meets WCAG AA as in Tokkl: 4.5:1 for text and 3:1 for non-text elements. Colour is never the only signal for a state. Every control has a TalkBack label, and layouts survive the largest font scale. Tokkl's sixth principle applies as written. Any style that costs a readable number, a reachable control or a passing test gets dropped.

The app is English only. All text lives in string resources. No other language is planned, and contributors can add translations.

[opaque value omitted]
---
type: practice
title: Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP
description: >-
  Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept
  any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning.
tags:
  - security
  - network
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to:
  - map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app connects to any Tokkl server, however it is set up. The Tokkl server itself listens on plain HTTP, and HTTPS comes from a reverse proxy or `tailscale serve` in front of it. Real servers use a public certificate, a certificate from `tailscale cert`, a self-signed certificate, or Tokkl's default of plain HTTP on port 8080.

HTTPS with a publicly trusted certificate or a CA the user installed in Android works (S2). For a self-signed certificate the user reads a disclaimer and trusts it. The app shows the certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint, trusts that certificate only, and asks again when it changes. It never accepts any certificate without a check (S3). The app pins the certificate itself, separate from Android's trust store.

Plain `http://` works only after a warning stronger than the self-signed one. The warning says the token travels unencrypted unless the network encrypts it, as Tailscale does (S4). Android 9 and later block cleartext unless the app opts in, and Android 7 and later trust only system CAs by default. So `res/xml/network_security_config.xml` permits cleartext and trusts system and user-added CAs. The self-signed pin stays out of that file and goes into the trust manager of `SharedHttpClient`, the app's only `OkHttpClient`.

[credential-related line omitted]

map-tokkl-toggl-api
---
type: map
title: 'Tokkl and Toggl API endpoints, auth and Tokkl-only extensions'
description: >-
  Tokkl speaks a subset of Toggl API v9 at /api/v9 and Reports v3 at
  /reports/api/v3 with Basic auth, plus Tokkl-only fields and endpoints.
tags:
  - api
  - toggl
  - network
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: map-tokkl-toggl-api
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
Tokkl implements a subset of the Toggl Track API v9 at `https://<server>/api/v9` and of the Reports API v3 at `https://<server>/reports/api/v3`. A client that accepts a base URL needs no other change to talk to it. The server URL may carry a path, such as `https://example.com/tokkl`. Toggl Track's API lives at `api.track.toggl.com`.

[credential-related line omitted]

Tokkl adds `role` and `time_resolution_minutes` to `GET /api/v9/me`, and the server snaps stopped entries to that resolution. Tokkl-only endpoints outside the Toggl API cover the trash (`GET/DELETE /api/app/trash`, `DELETE /api/app/trash/{id}`) and undoing a delete (`POST /api/app/time_entries/{id}/restore`). Favorites are a Tokkl key in `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

The first version targets Tokkl only. Toggl Track comes later by pointing the app at Toggl's base URL, and that version hides the Tokkl-only features.

uild slower than the previous one.
tags:
  - testing
  - ci
  - performance
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions
kk_derived_from:

The app has JVM unit tests, Compose UI tests, and instrumented tests on an emulator. Instrumented tests run against a real Tokkl server from the `ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl` image, never a fake one. `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh` starts that server and runs them.

The PWA sets the speed bar. If starting a timer is slower here, the owner drifts back to the PWA. Tests prove that a tap on Start or Stop changes the state with a network that never answers. Jetpack Macrobenchmark runs cold start (`StartupTimingMetric`) and tap-to-state (`TraceSectionMetric`) on the CI emulator and fails a build that is slower than the previous one.

The goals are 200 ms from tap to new state and 1.5 s cold start on a mid-range phone. No real phone checks them, so they guide design but do not gate releases.

kk_confidence: high
---
The emulator AVD is `tokkl-api36`, built on the plain AOSP image `system-images;android-36;default;x86_64` (Android 16). It is not a Google APIs, Play Store or Automated Test Device (ATD) image. ATD images are smaller, but they remove SystemUI and the launcher, and the notification, Quick Settings tile and widget tests need both.

Boot it without a window: `emulator -avd tokkl-api36 -no-window -no-audio -no-boot-anim -no-snapshot -accel on`. With `-accel on` the emulator refuses to start when KVM is missing, instead of falling back to slow software emulation. `emulator -accel-check` says whether KVM works.

A fresh headless boot often leaves a "System UI isn't responding" dialog that holds window focus, so injected keys go to the dialog instead of the app. `adb shell am broadcast -a android.intent.action.CLOSE_SYSTEM_DIALOGS` closes it, and tests call `DeviceShell.closeSystemDialogs()` in `app/src/androidTest`, which sends the same broadcast.

This machine is an always-on homelab server tuned for low idle power. When the tests finish, run `adb emu kill` and then `adb kill-server`, so neither the emulator nor the adb server keeps running. To look for leftovers, use `pgrep -x adb` and `pgrep -af qemu-system`. `pgrep -f adb` also matches `mariadbd` and the shell that runs the command.

---
type: practice
title: Build with --no-daemon in parallel worktrees and never run --stop
description: >-
  ./gradlew --stop ends every daemon of that Gradle version for the user,
  including one serving a build in another worktree.
tags:
  - build
  - gradle
  - agents
  - worktrees
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-gradle-no-daemon-in-parallel-worktrees
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to:
  - map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells
  - practice-run-npm-install-in-every-clone-and-worktree
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
Gradle daemons are shared by every project of the same user and Gradle version, through the registry in `~/.gradle/daemon/<version>`. `./gradlew --stop` therefore stops all of them, including the daemon of a build running in another git worktree, and that build dies. Never run `--stop` while another build may be running. When more than one worktree builds at the same time, run each build as `./gradlew --no-daemon <task>` so it gets its own single-use process and shares no daemon.

# Snap unsent entries on the phone with a Kotlin port of snapSpan

## Problem

PRD T3 now snaps entries that have not reached the server on the phone, with a port of `snapSpan` in the account timezone, so their times do not jump when the server answers (docs/PRD.md:105). Section 6.1 lists that port next to the parsing port, checked against the same cases as the TypeScript tests (docs/PRD.md:195). Risk 5 warns that a Kotlin port drifts from the original unless the two share test cases (docs/PRD.md:257). No ticket ports `snapSpan`, and TKA-0029 still says the phone never snaps times (.ai/sift/open/tracking/feature/TKA-0029--history.md:37). Without the port, an entry stopped offline shows raw times and then moves when the server returns snapped ones.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:105
- docs/PRD.md:195
- docs/PRD.md:257
- .ai/sift/open/tracking/feature/TKA-0029--history.md:37

## Direction

- Port `snapSpan` from `../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts` to Kotlin, in the same module as TKA-0012's parsing port. It takes start, stop, `time_resolution_minutes` and the account timezone (S7), with the same semantics as the TypeScript.
- Reuse the cases in `../tokkl/packages/common/snap.test.ts` the same way TKA-0012 reuses the parser cases, so drift from the TypeScript fails a test (risk 5).
- Before display, run it on every stopped entry the queue holds: entries stopped, created or edited offline. Never snap the running clock.
- Skip snapping when `/me` has no `time_resolution_minutes`. This is a capability check (PRD 8.3).
- Once the change is sent, the server's values replace the local ones.
- TKA-0029 will be edited separately to point here, so leave it alone in this ticket.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The Kotlin `snapSpan` passes every case from Tokkl's `snap.test.ts`.
- [ ] An entry stopped offline shows snapped start and stop before it is sent.
- [ ] For the shared cases, the start and stop shown after the server answers are identical to those shown before sending.
- [ ] The running clock is never snapped.
- [ ] With no `time_resolution_minutes` in `/me`, no entry is snapped.

---

# Resolve the account timezone from /me as leniently as Tokkl's Intl check

## Problem

Tokkl accepts any timezone name that `Intl.DateTimeFormat` accepts and stores it as sent. Intl matches names case-insensitively, so `europe/madrid` and `utc` pass and come back from `GET /api/v9/me` unchanged. `java.time.ZoneId.of` is case-sensitive and throws `ZoneRulesException` for both. The phone needs a `ZoneId` for the account timezone everywhere (TKA-0023) and for `snapSpan` and `snapUnsent`, so an account saved with such a name would fail on the phone while the web app works.

## Evidence

- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/togglV9.ts:150-151 checks `timezone` with `isValidTimeZone` only, and :187-190 stores it as sent.
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/app.ts:55 does the same for setup and invite forms.
- ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts:30-37 `isValidTimeZone` is "Intl accepts it".
- Node 24.21 accepted `europe/madrid`, `EUROPE/MADRID` and `utc`; `ZoneId.of` on Temurin 21.0.12 threw `ZoneRulesException` for all three (checked 2026-10-18).
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/time/Snap.kt takes a `ZoneId`.

## Direction

- Add a pure function in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time` that turns the `/me` timezone string into a `ZoneId`. Try `ZoneId.of` first, then match the name case-insensitively against `ZoneId.getAvailableZoneIds()`, mapping `utc` and similar to the canonical ID.
- Return null for a name neither accepts, so TKA-0023 can decide the fallback. Do not fall back to the device zone silently.
- Pin the names Intl accepts and `ZoneId.of` rejects with a JVM test, with expected values checked in Node.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `europe/madrid`, `EUROPE/MADRID` and `utc` resolve to `Europe/Madrid`, `Europe/Madrid` and a UTC zone.
- [ ] Canonical names such as `Europe/Madrid` and `Australia/Lord_Howe` resolve unchanged.
- [ ] A name Intl rejects returns null.

resolution: "TKA-0017: ConfiguredServerGuard on SharedHttpClient rejects any other scheme/host/port with ApiError.HostNotAllowed(origin) carrying no credentials; OkHttp redirects off, same-origin redirects followed by the guard; SingleHttpClientTest scans compiled classes for any other HTTP client."
---

# Refuse requests to any host other than the configured server

## Problem

Principle 1 keeps data between the phone and the server. The app sends no telemetry, analytics, crash reports or ads, and makes no request to any host other than the configured server. The privacy requirement repeats that no network traffic goes to any other host. Nothing in the app enforces this at runtime, so a stray request or a redirect could reach another host unnoticed.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:66
- docs/PRD.md:211
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- Add an interceptor to the shared OkHttp client from TKA-0009. It rejects any request whose scheme, host or port differs from the configured server's. During sign-in, the configured server is the URL the user entered.
[credential-related line omitted]
- Refuse redirects to another host as well. OkHttp follows redirects inside the client, past application interceptors, so turn off automatic redirects and follow one only when its target passes the same check.
- Add a check, such as a test or lint rule, that fails when app code builds an HTTP client other than the shared one.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A JVM test shows a request to the configured host passes and a request to another host fails with a typed error that names the host.
- [ ] The error carries no credentials.
- [ ] A redirect to another host is refused.
- [ ] A check confirms no other HTTP client in the app bypasses the shared one.

# Correct the sendNoContent KDoc about which Tokkl DELETE routes return an empty body

## Problem

The KDoc on `TogglApiClient.sendNoContent` says Tokkl's DELETE routes answer 200 with an empty body. That holds for time entries, tasks, clients and tags, but Tokkl answers a project DELETE with the project id as JSON. The method ignores any body, so behaviour is right; the comment is wrong. Someone writing the project delete later could read it and decode nothing, or "fix" the method to reject bodies.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/TogglApiClient.kt:41-45 says "such as Tokkl's DELETE routes, which answer 200 with an empty body".
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/togglV9.ts:397-401 answers `DELETE /workspaces/:wid/projects/:pid` with `res.json(parseId(req.params.pid))`.
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/togglV9.ts:370-374, and the task, client and tag DELETE routes at :454, :492 and :511, return `res.status(200).end()`.

## Direction

- Reword the KDoc: calls whose response body the caller does not need, such as Tokkl's DELETE of a time entry, task, client or tag (200 with an empty body). Note that a project DELETE returns the id, which `sendNoContent` ignores

# Make the release APK reproducible and signed with the project key from Actions secrets

## Problem

F-Droid publishes the APK signed with the project's key only when its own build matches. That needs pinned build-tools and no embedded timestamps. F-Droid is the only store, and the same signed APK on each GitHub release serves people who sideload. Signing with the project's own key also keeps Google developer registration possible in 2027. Nothing defines signing or pinning yet, and the key must never enter the repo.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:226 says F-Droid publishes the project-signed APK only when its own build matches, and asks for pinned build-tools and no embedded timestamps.
- docs/PRD.md:234 makes F-Droid the only store, with the signed APK on each GitHub release for sideloading.
- docs/PRD.md:245 has semantic-release attach the signed release APK to each GitHub release.
- docs/PRD.md:246 keeps the signing key in GitHub Actions secrets, never in the repo.
- docs/PRD.md:256 says signing with the project's own key keeps Google developer registration possible later.
- app/build.gradle.kts exists since TKA-0004 (commit f5c5cc4) with no signingConfig and no buildToolsVersion; TKA-0004 left build-tools pinning to this ticket.
- On 2026-10-18 the TKA-0004 worker saw the debug APK's hash differ between an incremental build and a clean rebuild of the same commit and did not find the cause (logs under /tmp/tka0004-logs/, may be gone).

## Direction

[credential-related line omitted]
- Add keystore files to `.gitignore`.
- Pin the build-tools and toolchain versions. Remove embedded timestamps and other sources of non-determinism.
- Add a script or CI step that builds the unsigned release APK twice from clean and compares the two.
- Document the secret names, how the owner generates the key, and the command that prints the certificate's SHA-256 for F-Droid's `AllowedAPKSigningKeys`.
- The owner generates the key and adds the secrets. This ticket does neither and commits no key material.
- The repo has no remote yet. CI signing can be checked only after the move to github.com/e0ipso/tokkl-android and after the owner creates the key.

## Acceptance criteria

[credential-related line omitted]
- [ ] Two clean release builds produce identical unsigned APKs.
- [ ] The build-tools version is pinned.
- [ ] Release signing reads only environment variables, and the docs list the secret names and the fingerprint command.
- [ ] Verified after the move to GitHub: once the owner has created the key and the secrets, CI signs the release APK.

resolution: "TKA-0099: scripts/instrumented-tests.sh boots the emulator with systemd-run --user --scope -p OOMPolicy=continue (PartOf the caller) and -memory 2048, plain child without a user manager; a killed emulator makes the script exit non-zero naming SIGKILL; full suite passes with qemu in its own tokkl-emulator scope; waived: fallback checked locally with the user bus hidden, not in real CI."
---

# Boot the harness emulator in its own systemd scope so an OOM kill cannot end the agent session

## Problem

`scripts/instrumented-tests.sh` starts the emulator as a child of whatever runs it. When an agent runs the script, qemu lands in that agent session's systemd scope (`t3code-claude-*.scope`), which has `OOMPolicy=stop`. On this 16 GB machine qemu holds 3 to 4 GB, and the kernel's OOM killer picks it first (`oom_score_adj` 800). systemd then stops the whole scope, which kills Claude Code and every background agent of the drain. That happened three times on 2026-10-18 and lost hours of agent time each time.

## Evidence

- `journalctl -k`: OOM kills of `qemu-system-x86` at 02:09:39 (anon-rss 3,991,664 kB), 11:19:20 (2,855,740 kB) and 13:15:47 (2,953,340 kB), each with `task_memcg=.../app-t3code-agents.slice/.../t3code-claude-<id>.scope`.
- `journalctl --user` at 02:09:40: `t3code-claude-6bf4c3bb.scope: Failed with result 'oom-kill'`, after 2 h 4 min of CPU time.
- `systemctl --user show 't3code-claude-*.scope' -p OOMPolicy` prints `OOMPolicy=stop`.
- scripts/instrumented-tests.sh:79 starts `emulator -avd ...` in the background of the script's own process tree.
- At 13:20 swap was full (4095/4095 MB) and the tmpfs `/tmp` held 6.4 GB.

## Direction

- When `systemd-run` is available and a user manager is running, start the emulator with `systemd-run --user --scope` (or `--unit` with `--collect`) and `-p OOMPolicy=continue`, so an OOM kill takes only qemu and the script fails cleanly instead of the caller's session dying. Fall back to the current launch when `systemd-run --user` is not usable, such as in CI.
- Cap the guest RAM explicitly (`-memory 2048` or the AVD's `hw.ramSize`) and record the measured qemu RSS before and after.
- Keep the cleanup trap working: the script must still stop the emulator it started, including when the scope or unit outlives

codex
The tickets record some deferred work, including displaying snapped offline entries and verifying signing after the GitHub move. I’ll account for those boundaries while reviewing the shipped code.

# Close the SystemUI ANR dialog after the harness boots the emulator

## Problem

A fresh headless boot of `tokkl-api36` often leaves an "Application Not Responding: com.android.systemui" dialog holding window focus. Any key the instrumented tests inject, such as system back, goes to that dialog instead of the app, so the test fails for a reason that has nothing to do with the app. The wave 3 e2e pass worked around it inside the tests with a `CLOSE_SYSTEM_DIALOGS` broadcast. Every later UI test that injects keys would need the same workaround, and a test that forgets it fails only on a fresh boot.

## Evidence

- The wave 3 e2e specialist saw `dumpsys window` report `mCurrentFocus=Window{… Application Not Responding: com.android.systemui}` after a fresh boot; pressing back by hand with the app focused worked.
- app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/DeviceShell.kt:20 sends `am broadcast -a android.intent.action.CLOSE_SYSTEM_DIALOGS` as the test-side workaround.
- The TKA-0004 and TKA-0085 workers also saw "System UI isn't responding" on this AVD.
- scripts/instrumented-tests.sh boots the AVD in `boot_emulator` and waits only for `sys.boot_completed`.

## Direction

- In `boot_emulator`, after `sys.boot_completed`, dismiss system dialogs once and keep error dialogs from taking focus on the AVD the script boots, for example `settings put global hide_error_dialogs 1` plus a `CLOSE_SYSTEM_DIALOGS` broadcast. Only change settings on an emulator the script booted itself, never on a device that was already attached.
- Then drop the per-test workaround if no test needs it any more, keeping `DeviceShell` if other tests use it.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Three consecutive fresh-boot runs of `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh` pass with no test-side dialog workaround.
- [ ] The script changes no setting on a device it did not boot.

SHA pins never move on their own, so the workflow actions go stale without notice. The PRD pairs SHA pinning with Dependabot, as Tokkl does. The Gradle dependencies and the npm commitlint tooling need the same updates. Nothing configures Dependabot, and it only runs once the repo is on GitHub.

- The repo has no remote yet. Write the file now. Checking that Dependabot opens update PRs waits for the move to github.com/e0ipso/tokkl-android.

## Acceptance criteria

resolution: "TKA-0086: .gitattributes from Gradle's template (gradlew eol=lf, *.bat eol=crlf, *.jar binary); gradlew.bat index blob normalized to LF, working-tree bytes unchanged; fresh clones under every core.autocrlf give LF/CRLF; renormalize leaves the index unchanged."
---

# Pin line endings for the Gradle wrapper scripts with .gitattributes

## Problem

TKA-0004 committed the Gradle wrapper with `gradlew.bat` in CRLF and `gradlew` in LF, but the repo has no `.gitattributes`. A checkout with `core.autocrlf` set can rewrite either script, and a `gradlew` with CRLF fails to run on Linux. `git diff --check` over any range that touches `gradlew.bat` also reports every line of it as trailing whitespace, which hides real whitespace errors in the same output. Gradle's own `gradle init` template ships a `.gitattributes` for exactly this.

## Evidence

- `file gradlew.bat` on 2026-10-18 reports "DOS batch file, ASCII text, with CRLF line terminators".
- `git diff --check d3cf48c HEAD` on 2026-10-18 exited 2 with 112 findings, all in gradlew.bat.
- absent: .gitattributes
- `git check-attr -a gradlew.bat` prints nothing.

## Direction

- Add `.gitattributes` with `gradlew text eol=lf`, `*.bat text eol=crlf` and `*.jar binary`, as Gradle's init template does.
- Check that `git add --renormalize .` changes no tracked file, so the commit holds only the new file.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `git check-attr eol gradlew gradlew.bat` reports `lf` and `crlf`.
- [ ] `git add --renormalize .` leaves the index unchanged.
- [ ] `./gradlew --version` still runs.

# Fail the instrumented run when the test device dies before every test ran

## Problem

When the emulator died in the middle of the instrumented tests, `./gradlew connectedDebugAndroidTest` printed BUILD SUCCESSFUL and exited 0 after 1 of the 32 tests. `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh` now turns that into a failure only for an emulator it booted itself, because it notices its own qemu is gone. On a device that was already attached, which is how the CI job from TKA-0019 runs, a crashed or OOM-killed emulator gives a green run that tested almost nothing.

## Expected behaviour

The script exits non-zero and says why when a target device goes offline or disappears before every test has run, whether the script booted it or not.

## Steps to reproduce

1. Run `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh` with no device attached.
2. When the log prints `Running tests on devices`, wait a few seconds and `kill -KILL` the `qemu-system-x86` process.
3. Gradle prints `adb: device offline` while uninstalling, then BUILD SUCCESSFUL; the result XML holds 1 test.

## Evidence

- TKA-0099 SIGKILL ch

# Run the reproducible release check in CI

## Problem

TKA-0020 added `scripts/reproducible-release.sh`, which builds the unsigned release APK in two fresh clones and compares them byte for byte. It runs only when someone starts it. A change that makes the release build unstable, such as a plugin that embeds a timestamp or a library whose dexing output varies, would land unnoticed until F-Droid fails to verify a release and stops publishing it. CI runs only `assembleDebug check` today.

## Evidence

- .github/workflows/ci.yml:36 runs `./gradlew assembleDebug check --stacktrace` and nothing that builds the release APK.
- docs/PRD.md:231 says F-Droid publishes the project-signed APK only when its own build matches.
- scripts/reproducible-release.sh (TKA-0020, branch sift/w4-a3) exits 1 when the two APKs differ. On 2026-10-18 it took 223 seconds on the build machine and used up to about 660 MB under `build/`.

## Direction

- Add a job to `ci.yml` that runs `scripts/reproducible-release.sh` on pull requests and pushes to `main`. TKA-0019 also edits `ci.yml`, so start after it lands.
- Reuse the `gradle` job's setup: Temurin 21, `platforms;android-37.0` and `build-tools;36.0.0`, every action pinned to a full commit SHA with its version in a comment, and `persist-credentials: false`.
- The script clones from the checkout's git directory. Check that it works with `actions/checkout`'s default shallow fetch, or set `fetch-depth: 0`.
- The job sets none of the `TOKKL_RELEASE_*` signing variables.
- The repo has no remote yet. Write the job now; checking that it goes green waits for the move to github.com/e0ipso/tokkl-android.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A `ci.yml` job runs `scripts/reproducible-release.sh` on pull requests and pushes to `main`, with every action pinned by SHA.
- [ ] The job fails when the two APKs differ.
- [ ] Verified after the move to GitHub: the job passes on `main`.

# Add ui-test-manifest and test the clock and totals styles at the largest font scale

## Problem

TKA-0008 requires the clock, the totals and the headings to survive the largest font scale without clipping. The headings are now covered on the launch and Licences screens, but no screen renders the clock or totals styles yet, and a Compose test cannot host a bare composable because the build lacks `ui-test-manifest`. Until the timer and totals screens exist, nothing checks those two styles, and the screens built on them later would inherit any clipping.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/Type.kt defines the clock and totals styles (TKA-0008).
- `grep -n 'ui-test' gradle/libs.versions.toml` finds only `androidx-compose-ui-test-junit4`; there is no `ui-test-manifest` entry and app/build.gradle.kts declares none.
- app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/ui/theme/LargestFontScaleTest.kt checks headlines on real screens at 200% and reports clock and totals as unreachable.

## Direction

- Add `androidx.compose.ui:ui-test-manifest` to the catalog (version from the Compose BOM) and as `debugImplementation` in app/build.gradle.kts.
- Add a Compose UI test that hosts a sample clock text and a sample totals text in `TokklTheme` at the largest font scale the device offers and at a narrow width, and checks they neither clip in height nor overlap. Use the realistic longest strings, such as `23:59:59` and a multi-digit total.
- Check that `./gradlew check` still passes the denied-dependency check (TKA-0005).

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `ui-test-manifest` is a `debugImplementation` dependency from the Compose BOM.
- [ ] A test renders the clock and totals styles at the largest font scale and passes through `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh`.
- [ ] Replacing the clock style with one that clips makes the test fail.

- docs/PRD.md:177 defines G2. Opt-in dynamic color.
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

Add a settings screen. TKA-0038 later adds sign-out to it.

The screen has a theme mode of light, dark or follow system, with follow system as the default. It also has a dynamic colour switch, off by default and shown only on Android 12 and later. Persist both on the phone. The theme from TKA-0007 reads them, so a change applies at once without a restart.

With dynamic colour off, the app uses Tokkl's palette in every mode. With it on, the app uses the wallpaper colours in the chosen mode.

All text lives in English string resources, every control has a TalkBack label. The screen stays usable at the largest font scale.

- [ ] Dynamic colour is off by default and hidden below Android 12.

+## Release builds
+
+F-Droid builds the app from source and compares its unsigned APK with the signed APK on the GitHub release. It publishes the APK signed with the project's key only when the two match apart from the signature ([reproducible builds](https://f-droid.org/docs/Reproducible_Builds/)). People who sideload install the same APK from the GitHub release.
+
+### Check that the release build is reproducible
+
+```sh
+scripts/reproducible-release.sh          # HEAD
+scripts/reproducible-release.sh v0.1.0   # a tag or any other commit
+```
+
+The script clones the commit twice, runs `./gradlew --no-daemon assembleRelease` in each clone without the signing variables below, and compares the two unsigned APKs byte for byte. It builds committed work only. The clones sit at paths of different lengths. The second build runs on at most two CPU cores and gets its own Gradle user home, which reads downloaded dependencies from yours but redoes every transform. So an embedded path, output that depends on the core count, and a transform that differs from run to run all show up as differences. When the APKs differ, the script lists the zip entries that differ and keeps both APKs. On this machine the two builds take about four minutes and use up to about 700 MB under `build/`, which the script removes at the end. CI runs the script on pull requests to `main` and on pushes to `main`.
+
+These keep the APK the same from build to build:
+
+- `app/build.gradle.kts` pins Build-Tools 36.0.0. The wrapper pins Gradle 9.8.1 by checksum, and `gradle/libs.versions.toml` pins the Android Gradle Plugin, Kotlin and every library. CI builds with Temurin JDK 21, a version F-Droid's build server also offers.
+- Every zip entry carries the fixed date 1981-01-01 and no extra timestamp field.

+- The manifest records `platformBuildVersionName='17'` and `platformBuildVersionCode='37'`, which come from the `platforms;android-37.0` package, revision 2 here. Gradle cannot pin a platform revision, so a newer revision on F-Droid's server could change them.
+- The Android Gradle Plugin writes the commit hash, and no path, into `META-INF/version-control-info.textproto`. F-Droid asks to keep that file and to build the release APK from a clean checkout of the tagged commit. So build the GitHub release APK once the release commit and its tag exist, never from a tree with uncommitted changes, such as one where the release step has just rewritten `gradle/version.properties`.
+- The ART baseline profile, `assets/dexopt/baseline.prof` and `baseline.profm`, stays in the APK because it speeds up start-up and came out the same on 8 and on 2 cores. F-Droid's docs say it is sometimes unstable. If the script ever shows it differing, turn off the tasks whose names contain `ArtProfile`, as those docs describe.
+- `dependenciesInfo` is off. Otherwise the Android Gradle Plugin adds the dependency list to the signing block of the signed APK, encrypted with a key only Google holds, and F-Droid fails a build whose APK carries that block.
+
+### Sign the release APK
+
+Release signing reads four environment variables and nothing else:
+
+| Variable | Value |
+| --- | --- |
+| `TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE` | Path of the keystore file. Use an absolute path; a relative one counts from `app/`. |
[credential-related line omitted]
+| `TOKKL_RELEASE_KEY_ALIAS` | Alias of the key in the keystore |
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+With all four set, `./gradlew assembleRelease` writes the signed `app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk`. With none set, it writes `app-release-unsigned.apk` instead. With only some set, the build fails and names the missing ones. An empty variable counts as not set, which is what Actions passes for a secret that does not exist.
+
+The owner creates the key once, outside the repo:
+
+```sh
+keytool -genkeypair -keystore ~/tokkl-release.p12 -storetype PKCS12 \
+  -alias tokkl -keyalg RSA -keysize 4096 -validity 10000
+```
+
[credential-related line omitted]
+
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+```sh
+base64 -w0 ~/tokkl-release.p12 | gh secret set TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64
[credential-related line omitted]
+gh secret set TOKKL_RELEASE_KEY_ALIAS --body tokkl
[credential-related line omitted]
+```
+
+The release job writes the keystore to the runner's temporary directory and points `TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE` at it. No workflow has this step yet; the release workflow adds it once the repo is on GitHub:
+
+```yaml
+- name: Build the signed release APK
+  env:
+    TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64: ${{ secrets.TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64 }}
[credential-related line omitted]
+    TOKKL_RELEASE_KEY_ALIAS: ${{ secrets.TOKKL_RELEASE_KEY_ALIAS }}
[credential-related line omitted]
+  run: |
+    export TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE="$RUNNER_TEMP/release.p12"
+    printf '%s' "$TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64" | base64 -d >"$TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE"
+    ./gradlew assembleRelease
+```
+
+The Android Gradle Plugin signs the APK itself. Don't sign it again with `apksigner` from Build-Tools 35 or later: F-Droid's docs say its signature copy check fails on APKs signed that way.
+
+F-Droid's `AllowedAPKSigningKeys` takes the SHA-256 fingerprint of the signing certificate in lower-case hex. This prints it from a signed APK:
+
+```sh
+"$ANDROID_HOME/build-tools/36.0.0/apksigner" verify --print-certs \
+  app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk | grep 'SHA-256'
+```
+
+Before any APK exists, keytool prints the same value from the keystore:
+
+```sh
+keytool -list -v -keystore ~/tokkl-release.p12 -alias tokkl \
+  | sed -n 's/^[[:space:]]*SHA256: //p' | tr -d ':' | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]'
+```
+
+F-Droid checks a release by copying the signature from the GitHub APK onto its own unsigned build with [apksigcopier](https://github.com/obfusk/apksigcopier). This runs the same check locally:
+
+```sh
+apksigcopier compare --unsigned app-release.apk app-release-unsigned.apk
+```
+
 ## Commit messages
 
 Commit messages follow [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/). `npm install` sets up a commit-msg hook that runs commitlint, which needs Node 22.12 or later. The hook lives in `.husky/_`, which git ignores, so run `npm install` once in every new clone or git worktree. Until you do, commits there skip the check. The hook rejects a ticket ID such as `TKA-0001` in the subject; put it in a `Refs: TKA-0001` trailer instead. It also rejects commits that credit an AI tool or a co-author (`Co-authored-by:`, "Generated with Claude Code" and the like). `npm test` checks these rules, and runs the hook against real commits and merges in a throwaway repository. The local hook never sees a commit made with `git revert` or `--no-verify`, so the commitlint job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` also lints the commits of every pull request and push to `main`, and runs `npm test`.

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl
+
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.ComposeTestRule
+import androidx.compose.ui.test.onAllNodesWithText
+
+/**
+ * Waits until a node with [text] exists. The first screen appears only after
+ * the app has read the stored session off the main thread, which the compose
+ * rule's idle check does not wait for.
+ */
+fun ComposeTestRule.awaitText(text: String, timeoutMillis: Long = START_TIMEOUT_MS) {
+    waitUntil("\"$text\" shows", timeoutMillis) { onAllNodesWithText(text).fetchSemanticsNodes().isNotEmpty() }
+}
+
+private const val START_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000L

+/**
+ * Each theme mode survives a restart of the app process. The steps run in name order, and the
+ * test orchestrator (app/build.gradle.kts) starts a new process for each. Every step after the
+ * first starts the app, checks the colours and the choice the step before left, and picks the
+ * next mode. A step fails if it runs in the same process as the one before.
+ */
+@FixMethodOrder(MethodSorters.NAME_ASCENDING)
+class ThemeSettingsRestartTest {
+    @get:Rule(order = 0)
+    val session = SessionRule()
+
+    @get:Rule(order = 1)
+    val composeRule = createEmptyComposeRule()
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+
+    /** Names the process that ran the step before. */
+    private val previousStep = File(context.cacheDir, "ThemeSettingsRestartTest.process")
+
+    @Test
+    fun step1ChoosesLight() {
+        ThemeSettingsStore(context).clear()
+        previousStep.delete()
+        launch { activity ->
+            choose(ThemeMode.LIGHT)
+            assertEquals(tokklTheme(dark = false), composeRule.shownTheme(activity))
+        }
+        endStep(ThemeMode.LIGHT)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step2KeepsLightAndChoosesDark() {
+        assertNewProcess()
+        launch { activity ->
+            assertEquals("on start", tokklTheme(dark = false), composeRule.shownTheme(activity))
+            composeRule.openSettings()
+            mode(ThemeMode.LIGHT).assertIsSelected()
+            choose(ThemeMode.DARK)
+        }
+        endStep(ThemeMode.DARK)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step3KeepsDarkAndChoosesTheSystem() {
+        assertNewProcess()
+        launch { activity ->
+            assertEquals("on start", tokklTheme(dark = true), composeRule.shownTheme(activity))
+            composeRule.openSettings()
+            mode(ThemeMode.DARK).assertIsSelected()
+            choose(ThemeMode.SYSTEM)
+        }
+        endStep(ThemeMode.SYSTEM)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step4KeepsFollowingTheSystem() {
+        try {
+            assertNewProcess()
+            launch { activity ->

 succeeded in 0ms:
CHUNK 7 OF 36
+                assertEquals("on start", tokklTheme(dark = systemIsDark(activity)), composeRule.shownTheme(activity))
+                composeRule.openSettings()
+                mode(ThemeMode.SYSTEM).assertIsSelected()
+            }
+        } finally {
+            ThemeSettingsStore(context).clear()
+            previousStep.delete()
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Starts the app on the launch screen and runs [steps] with its activity. */
+    private fun launch(steps: (Activity) -> Unit) {
+        ActivityScenario.launch(MainActivity::class.java).use { scenario ->
+            composeRule.awaitText(string(R.string.launch_message))
+            lateinit var activity: Activity
+            scenario.onActivity { activity = it }
+            steps(activity)
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Picks [mode], opening the settings screen first if needed. */
+    private fun choose(mode: ThemeMode) {
+        if (composeRule.onAllNodes(hasText(string(R.string.settings_theme))).fetchSemanticsNodes().isEmpty()) {
+            composeRule.openSettings()
+        }
+        mode(mode).performClick().assertIsSelected()
+    }
+
+    private fun mode(mode: ThemeMode) = composeRule.onNode(hasText(string(THEME_MODE_LABELS.getValue(mode))) and isSelectable())
+
+    /** Waits for [chosen] to reach the disk, then names this process for the next step. */
+    private fun endStep(chosen: ThemeMode) {
+        awaitModeOnDisk(chosen)
+        previousStep.writeText(currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    private fun assertNewProcess() {
+        assertTrue("the step before did not run", previousStep.exists())
+        assertNotEquals("the step before ran in this same process", previousStep.readText(), currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    /** A PID can be reused, so the process start time goes with it. */
+    private fun currentProcess(): String = "${Process.myPid()}@${Process.getStartElapsedRealtime()}"
+
+    private fun tokklTheme(dark: Boolean): ShownTheme =
+        expectedTheme(if (dark) TokklDarkColorScheme.background else TokklLightColorScheme.background, dark)
+}

+private val targetContext get() = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext

+/**
+ * Puts the theme settings back to their defaults before and after each test. Order it before
+ * the rule that starts the activity, which reads the settings once per process.
+ */
+class DefaultThemeSettings : ExternalResource() {
+    override fun before() = ThemeSettingsStore(targetContext).clear()
+
+    override fun after() = ThemeSettingsStore(targetContext).clear()
+}
+
+fun string(id: Int): String = targetContext.getString(id)
+
+/** The label of each theme mode on the settings screen. */
+val THEME_MODE_LABELS = mapOf(
+    ThemeMode.SYSTEM to R.string.settings_theme_system,
+    ThemeMode.LIGHT to R.string.settings_theme_light,
+    ThemeMode.DARK to R.string.settings_theme_dark,
+)
+
+/** Opens the settings screen from the launch screen. */
+fun ComposeTestRule.openSettings() {
+    awaitText(string(R.string.settings))
+    onNodeWithText(string(R.string.settings)).performClick()
+    awaitText(string(R.string.settings_theme))
+}
+
+/**
+ * What the screen shows: the colour of the content at the left edge, half way down, where every
+ * screen shows only its Scaffold's background; the window's background; and whether the system
+ * bars draw dark icons for a light background.
+ */
+data class ShownTheme(val content: String, val window: String?, val lightStatusBar: Boolean)
+
+fun ComposeTestRule.shownTheme(activity: Activity): ShownTheme {
+    val pixels = onRoot().captureToImage().toPixelMap()
+    val content = pixels[0, pixels.height / 2].toArgb()
+    var window: Int? = null
+    var lightStatusBar = false
+    InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().runOnMainSync {
+        window = (activity.window.decorView.background as? ColorDrawable)?.color
+        lightStatusBar = WindowInsetsControllerCompat(activity.window, activity.window.decorView).isAppearanceLightStatusBars
+    }
+    return ShownTheme(hex(content), window?.let(::hex), lightStatusBar)
+}
+
+/** The theme a scheme [background] should produce, as [shownTheme] reports it. */
+fun expectedTheme(background: Color, dark: Boolean): ShownTheme =
+    hex(background.toArgb()).let { ShownTheme(content = it, window = it, lightStatusBar = !dark) }
+
+fun systemIsDark(activity: Activity): Boolean =
+    (activity.resources.configuration.uiMode and Configuration.UI_MODE_NIGHT_MASK) == Configuration.UI_MODE_NIGHT_YES
+
+/**
+ * Waits until the preferences file on disk holds [mode], so a test that ends now leaves the
+ * choice for the next process to read.
+ */
+fun awaitModeOnDisk(mode: ThemeMode, timeoutMillis: Long = 5_000) {
+    val file = File(targetContext.dataDir, "shared_prefs/${ThemeSettingsStore.FILE_NAME}.xml")
+    val deadline = System.nanoTime() + timeoutMillis * 1_000_000
+    while (!(file.exists() && file.readText().contains(">${mode.name}<"))) {
+        check(System.nanoTime() < deadline) { "$file does not hold $mode" }
+        Thread.sleep(50)
+    }
+}
+
+private fun hex(argb: Int): String = "#%08X".format(argb)

+/**
+ * Checks the clock and totals styles at Android's largest font scale before any screen uses
+ * them. The longest realistic clock and a multi-digit total sit one under the other in a narrow
+ * column. Every glyph must show in full, and neither sample may overlap the other.
+ *
+ * The column is narrower than either sample, so both wrap. A style's line height matters only
+ * between lines. Compose keeps the font's own ascent above the first line and its descent below
+ * the last, so a single line keeps its glyphs whatever line height the style sets. Each line's
+ * glyphs have to stay inside that line's box. A line height shorter than the glyphs fails,
+ * because neighbouring lines then run into each other.
+ *
+ * Both samples share one layout. Each check draws one sample and leaves the other transparent,
+ * so every inked pixel belongs to the sample under test.
+ */
+class ClockAndTotalStylesFitTest {
+    private val composeRule = createComposeRule()
+
+    /** The font scale is set before the test activity starts, so it never has to restart. */
+    @get:Rule
+    val rules: RuleChain = RuleChain.outerRule(SystemFontScale(SystemFontScale.LARGEST)).around(composeRule)
+
+    private var inked by mutableStateOf(CLOCK)
+    private val layouts = mutableMapOf<String, TextLayoutResult>()
+    private var fontScale = 0f
+
+    @Test
+    fun clockAndTotalFitInANarrowColumn() {
+        composeRule.setContent {
+            fontScale = LocalDensity.current.fontScale
+            TokklTheme {
+                // The margin around the column shows glyphs that reach past their box.
+                Box(Modifier.testTag(HOST).background(MaterialTheme.colorScheme.background).padding(MARGIN)) {
+                    Column(Modifier.width(NARROW_WIDTH)) {
+                        Sample(CLOCK, CLOCK_SAMPLE, TokklTheme.clockTextStyle)
+                        Sample(TOTAL, TOTAL_SAMPLE, TokklTheme.totalTextStyle)
+                    }
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        composeRule.waitForIdle()
+        assertEquals("font scale", SystemFontScale.LARGEST, fontScale)
+
+        for (sample in listOf(CLOCK, TOTAL)) {
+            inked = sample
+            composeRule.waitForIdle()
+            val layout = layouts.getValue(sample)
+            assertTrue("$sample fits on one line, so its line height goes untested", layout.lineCount > 1)
+            assertFalse("$sample layout overflows its height", layout.didOverflowHeight)
+            assertGlyphsInsideTheirLines(sample, bounds(sample), layout)
+        }
+        assertFalse("$CLOCK overlaps $TOTAL", bounds(CLOCK).overlaps(bounds(TOTAL)))
+    }
+
+    @Composable
+    private fun Sample(tag: String, text: String, style: TextStyle) {
+        Text(
+            text = text,
+            style = style,
+            color = if (inked == tag) MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onBackground else Color.Transparent,
+            onTextLayout = { layouts[tag] = it },
+            modifier = Modifier.testTag(tag),
+        )
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Every pixel that differs from the margin's colour lies inside [box]. The rows that hold
+     * such pixels form one band per line, and each band lies inside its line's box. The samples
+     * are digits and colons, so a gap of empty rows separates the glyphs of lines that keep
+     * apart.
+     */
+    private fun assertGlyphsInsideTheirLines(sample: String, box: Rect, layout: TextLayoutResult) {
+        val host = bounds(HOST)
+        val pixels = composeRule.onNodeWithTag(HOST).captureToImage().toPixelMap()
+        val background = pixels[0, 0]
+        val outside = mutableListOf<Pair<Int, Int>>()
+        val inkedRows = (0 until pixels.height).filter { y ->
+            var inked = false
+            for (x in 0 until pixels.width) {
+                if (pixels[x, y] == background) continue
+                inked = true
+                // The centre of the pixel, in window coordinates like the boxes.
+                if (!box.containsPoint(host.left + x + 0.5f, host.top + y + 0.5f)) outside += x to y
+            }
+            inked
+        }
+        assertTrue(

+            val last = runs.lastOrNull()

+    /**
+     * Scrolls to each node in turn, which then has to show in full: inside the screen and, for
+     * text, not cut off. Then no two [blocks] overlap.
+     */
+    private fun assertFitWhenScrolledTo(
+        blocks: List<Pair<String, SemanticsNodeInteraction>>,
+        texts: List<Pair<String, SemanticsNodeInteraction>>,
+    ) {
+        val screen = composeRule.onRoot().getBoundsInRoot()
+        for ((name, node) in blocks + texts) {
+            node.performScrollTo()
+            node.assertIsDisplayed()
+            node.textLayout()?.let { assertFalse("text is cut off: $name", it.didOverflowHeight) }
+            val box = node.getUnclippedBoundsInRoot()
+            val inside = box.left >= screen.left && box.top >= screen.top &&
+                box.right <= screen.right && box.bottom <= screen.bottom
+            assertTrue("does not fit on the screen: $name", inside)
+        }
+        // Unclipped bounds share one scroll position, so they compare even off screen.
+        val bounds = blocks.map { (name, node) -> name to node.getUnclippedBoundsInRoot() }
+        for ((i, first) in bounds.withIndex()) {
+            for (second in bounds.drop(i + 1)) {
+                assertFalse("${first.first} overlaps ${second.first}", first.second.overlaps(second.second))
+            }
+        }
+    }
