+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme
+
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.DeviceShell
+import org.junit.rules.ExternalResource
+
+/**
+ * Sets the device's font scale, waits until the app's configuration has it, and puts the
+ * previous setting back afterwards, again waiting for the app to see it so the next test starts
+ * at the usual scale. Order it before the rule that starts the activity, so the activity never
+ * has to restart.
+ */
+class SystemFontScale(private val scale: Float) : ExternalResource() {
+    private var previous: String = NOT_SET
+
+    override fun before() {
+        previous = DeviceShell.run("settings get system font_scale").trim()
+        DeviceShell.run("settings put system font_scale $scale")
+        awaitAppFontScale(scale)
+    }
+
+    override fun after() {
+        if (previous == NOT_SET) {
+            DeviceShell.run("settings delete system font_scale")
+        } else {
+            DeviceShell.run("settings put system font_scale $previous")
+        }
+        awaitAppFontScale(previous.toFloatOrNull() ?: 1f)
+    }
+
+    private fun awaitAppFontScale(expected: Float) {
+        val resources = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext.resources
+        val deadline = System.nanoTime() + CONFIGURATION_TIMEOUT_NS
+        while (resources.configuration.fontScale != expected) {
+            check(System.nanoTime() < deadline) {
+                "the app's font scale is ${resources.configuration.fontScale}, not $expected"
+            }
+            Thread.sleep(POLL_MS)
+        }
+    }
+
+    companion object {
+        /** The largest step of Android's font size setting since Android 14. */
+        const val LARGEST = 2f
+
+        /** What `settings get` prints for a setting that was never written. */
+        private const val NOT_SET = "null"
+        private const val CONFIGURATION_TIMEOUT_NS = 10_000_000_000L
+        private const val POLL_MS = 50L
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl
+
+import android.app.Application
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ConfiguredServer
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerConfigStore

+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings.ThemeSettingsStore
+import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
+import kotlinx.coroutines.MainScope
+
+class TokklApplication : Application() {
+
+    /**
+     * Loaded on first use, which is MainActivity's onCreate, on the main thread. The first frame
+     * has to know the theme, or launch would show the other mode's colours first.
+     */
+    val themeSettings: ThemeSettingsModel by lazy {
+        val store = ThemeSettingsStore(this)
+        ThemeSettingsModel(initial = store.load(), save = store::save, scope = MainScope(), io = Dispatchers.IO)
+    }
+
+    override fun onCreate() {
+        super.onCreate()
+        // Every process, whatever starts it, sends requests only to the stored server.
+        ConfiguredServer.readStoredFrom(ServerConfigStore(this)::load)
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl
+
+import androidx.activity.compose.BackHandler
+import androidx.compose.runtime.Composable
+import androidx.compose.runtime.LaunchedEffect
+import androidx.compose.runtime.saveable.listSaver
+import androidx.compose.runtime.saveable.rememberSaveable
+import androidx.compose.runtime.snapshots.SnapshotStateList
+import androidx.compose.runtime.toMutableStateList
+import androidx.compose.ui.platform.LocalContext
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.licences.LicencesScreen
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.Session
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings.SettingsScreen
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings.ThemeSettingsModel
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.signin.SignInModel
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.signin.SignInScreen
+import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
+import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
+
+/** The app's screens. */
+enum class Destination { SIGN_IN, HOME, SETTINGS, LICENCES }
+
+/**
+ * The app's navigation host: a back stack of [Destination]s that survives
+ * configuration changes and process death. It starts at sign-in until a
+ * server and its token are stored, and at the home screen after that.
+ * System back pops the stack and closes the app from its first screen.
+ */
+@Composable
+fun TokklNavHost(signInModel: SignInModel, themeSettings: ThemeSettingsModel) {
+    val appContext = LocalContext.current.applicationContext
+    val backStack = rememberSaveable(saver = BackStackSaver) { SnapshotStateList<Destination>() }
+    if (backStack.isEmpty()) {
+        // Shows the window background, which matches the theme, while the
+        // stores are read off the main thread.
+        LaunchedEffect(Unit) {
+            val signedIn = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) { Session(appContext).isSignedIn() }
+            backStack.add(if (signedIn) Destination.HOME else Destination.SIGN_IN)
+        }
+        return
+    }
+    BackHandler(enabled = backStack.size > 1) { backStack.removeAt(backStack.lastIndex) }
+    when (backStack.last()) {
+        Destination.SIGN_IN -> SignInScreen(
+            model = signInModel,
+            onSignedIn = {
+                signInModel.reset()
+                backStack.clear()
+                backStack.add(Destination.HOME)
+            },
+        )
+        Destination.HOME -> LaunchScreen(
+            onOpenSettings = { backStack.add(Destination.SETTINGS) },
+            onOpenLicences = { backStack.add(Destination.LICENCES) },
+        )
+        Destination.SETTINGS -> SettingsScreen(
+            model = themeSettings,
+            onBack = { backStack.removeAt(backStack.lastIndex) },
+        )
+        Destination.LICENCES -> LicencesScreen(onBack = { backStack.removeAt(backStack.lastIndex) })
+    }
+}
+
+private val BackStackSaver = listSaver<SnapshotStateList<Destination>, String>(
+    save = { stack -> stack.map { it.name } },
+    restore = { names -> names.map(Destination::valueOf).toMutableStateList() },
+)

     /** A 2xx response whose body is not the expected JSON. */

+     * which is not the configured server, so nothing was sent there. Carries
+     * no credential.
+     */
+    data class HostNotAllowed(val origin: String) : ApiError
 }

+
+/**
+ * The one server the app may send requests to (docs/PRD.md principle 1).
+ * [SharedHttpClient] refuses every request and every redirect whose scheme,
+ * host or port differs from [current].
+ */
+object ConfiguredServer {
+
+    @Volatile
+    private var stored: () -> ServerUrl? = { null }
+
+    @Volatile
+    private var signInCandidate: ServerUrl? = null
+
+    private val signInLock = Any()
+
+    /**
+     * During sign-in, the URL being tried. Otherwise the stored server. Null
+     * when there is neither, which refuses every request.
+     */
+    val current: ServerUrl?
+        get() = signInCandidate ?: stored()
+
+    /** Where [current] reads the stored server from. The app sets this once at start. */
+    fun readStoredFrom(source: () -> ServerUrl?) {
+        stored = source
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Runs [attempt] with [candidate] as the configured server, since sign-in
+     * talks to a server before the app has stored it. One attempt runs at a
+     * time, and the stored server applies again when it returns.
+     */
+    fun <T> signingIn(candidate: ServerUrl, attempt: () -> T): T =
+        synchronized(signInLock) {
+            signInCandidate = candidate
+            try {
+                attempt()
+            } finally {
+                signInCandidate = null
+            }
+        }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import java.io.IOException
+import java.net.ProtocolException
+import okhttp3.HttpUrl
+import okhttp3.Interceptor
+import okhttp3.Request

+/**
+ * The guard refused a request, or a redirect, to [origin] (`scheme://host:port`),
+ * which is not the configured server. Nothing was sent there. The message
+ * names the origin only: no path, query or credential.
+ */
+class HostNotAllowedException(val origin: String) :
+    IOException("Refused a request to $origin, which is not the configured server")
+
+/**
+ * Lets requests reach the configured server only. It checks the request
+ * before OkHttp connects, and it follows redirects itself: OkHttp would
+ * follow them inside the call, past any application interceptor, so

 c+=l
chunks.append(c)

PY" in /home/e0ipso/workspace/apps/tokkl-android--review-w4
 succeeded in 0ms:
CHUNK 15 OF 36
+ * [SharedHttpClient] turns automatic redirects off. A redirect to the same
+ * origin is followed, with OkHttp's rules for the method and body. A redirect
+ * anywhere else fails with [HostNotAllowedException].
+ */
+internal class ConfiguredServerGuard(private val configured: () -> ServerUrl?) : Interceptor {
+
+    override fun intercept(chain: Interceptor.Chain): Response {
+        // One server for the whole call, redirects included.
+        val server = configured()
+        var request = chain.request()
+        requireAllowed(server, request.url)
+        var followUps = 0
+        while (true) {
+            val response = chain.proceed(request)
+            val next = redirect(request, response) ?: return response
+            response.close()
+            requireAllowed(server, next.url)
+            if (++followUps > MAX_FOLLOW_UPS) throw ProtocolException("Too many redirects: $followUps")
+            request = next
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun requireAllowed(server: ServerUrl?, url: HttpUrl) {
+        if (server == null || !server.isSameOrigin(url)) throw HostNotAllowedException(origin(url))
+    }
+
+    /** The request OkHttp would send for a redirect, or null when [response] is not one it follows. */
+    private fun redirect(request: Request, response: Response): Request? {
+        if (response.code !in REDIRECT_CODES) return null
+        val location = response.header("Location") ?: return null
+        val url = request.url.resolve(location) ?: return null
+        val builder = request.newBuilder().url(url)
+        val keepsMethod = response.code == HTTP_TEMPORARY_REDIRECT || response.code == HTTP_PERMANENT_REDIRECT
+        if (request.method != "GET" && request.method != "HEAD" && !keepsMethod) {
+            builder.method("GET", null)
+                .removeHeader("Transfer-Encoding")
+                .removeHeader("Content-Length")
+                .removeHeader("Content-Type")
+        }
+        return builder.build()
+    }
+
+    private companion object {
+        const val HTTP_TEMPORARY_REDIRECT = 307
+        const val HTTP_PERMANENT_REDIRECT = 308
+        val REDIRECT_CODES = setOf(300, 301, 302, 303, HTTP_TEMPORARY_REDIRECT, HTTP_PERMANENT_REDIRECT)
+
+        /** OkHttp's own limit. */
+        const val MAX_FOLLOW_UPS = 20
+
+        fun origin(url: HttpUrl): String {
+            val host = if (':' in url.host) "[${url.host}]" else url.host
+            return "${url.scheme}://$host:${url.port}"
+        }
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import android.content.Context
+import android.content.SharedPreferences
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * The configured server's base URL, in the app's private preferences. The URL
[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+ *
+ * The first call loads the preferences file, so make calls off the main thread.
+ */
+class ServerConfigStore(context: Context) {
+
+    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }
+
+    /** The stored server, or null when there is none or it no longer parses. */
+    fun load(): ServerUrl? = preferences.getString(KEY_BASE_URL, null)?.let(ServerUrl::parse)
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun save(server: ServerUrl) {
+        commit { putString(KEY_BASE_URL, server.toString()) }
+    }
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun clear() {
+        commit { remove(KEY_BASE_URL) }
+    }
+
+    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
+        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
+    }
+
+    internal companion object {
+        const val FILE_NAME = "server_config"
+        private const val KEY_BASE_URL = "base_url"
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import android.content.Context
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
[credential-related line omitted]
+import java.io.IOException
+import java.security.GeneralSecurityException
+
+/**
+ * The account the app is signed in to: the configured server and its API token.
[credential-related line omitted]
+ *
+ * Calls do disk and Keystore I/O, so make them off the main thread.
+ */
+class Session(
+    private val servers: ServerConfigStore,
[credential-related line omitted]
+) {
+
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    /**
+     * Signed in means a server is stored and the token decrypts. A Keystore or
+     * disk failure counts as signed out, so the user can sign in again.
+     */
+    fun isSignedIn(): Boolean =
+        try {
+            servers.load() != null && tokens.load() != null
+        } catch (e: GeneralSecurityException) {
+            false
+        } catch (e: IOException) {
+            false
+        }
+
+    /**
+     * Stores [server] as the configured server and [token] as its API token.
+     * The server is cleared first and written last, so a failure part way
+     * leaves the app signed out instead of pairing a token with another server.
+     */
+    @Throws(GeneralSecurityException::class, IOException::class)
+    fun save(server: ServerUrl, token: String) {
+        servers.clear()
+        tokens.save(token)
+        servers.save(server)
+    }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings
+
+import android.os.Build
+
+/** Light, dark, or whatever the system uses (docs/PRD.md G1). */
+enum class ThemeMode {
+    SYSTEM,
+    LIGHT,
+    DARK,
+    ;
+
+    /** Whether the app draws in dark colours, given whether the system is dark now. */
+    fun isDark(systemIsDark: Boolean): Boolean =
+        when (this) {
+            SYSTEM -> systemIsDark
+            LIGHT -> false
+            DARK -> true
+        }
+}

+    private val save: (ThemeSettings) -> Unit,
+    private val scope: CoroutineScope,
+    private val io: CoroutineContext,
+) {
+
+    private val mutableState = MutableStateFlow(initial)
+    val state: StateFlow<ThemeSettings> = mutableState.asStateFlow()
+
+    private val writes = Mutex()
+
+    /** What the disk holds. Read and written only while holding [writes]. */
+    private var saved = initial
+
+    fun onModeChange(mode: ThemeMode) = change { it.copy(mode = mode) }
+
+    fun onDynamicColorChange(on: Boolean) = change { it.copy(dynamicColor = on) }
+
+    private fun change(edit: (ThemeSettings) -> ThemeSettings) {
+        mutableState.update(edit)
+        scope.launch {
+            writes.withLock {
+                val wanted = mutableState.value
+                if (wanted == saved) return@withLock
+                try {
+                    withContext(io) { save(wanted) }
+                    saved = wanted
+                } catch (e: IOException) {
+                    mutableState.compareAndSet(wanted, saved)
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings
+
+import android.content.Context
+import android.content.SharedPreferences
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * The theme settings, in the app's private preferences like
+ * [com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerConfigStore]. They stay on the phone: the backup and
+ * data extraction rules leave out every domain.
+ *
+ * The first call loads the preferences file from disk.
+ */
+class ThemeSettingsStore(context: Context) {
+
+    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }
+
+    /** The stored settings, with the defaults for anything missing or unknown. */
+    fun load(): ThemeSettings {
+        val defaults = ThemeSettings()
+        val mode = preferences.getString(KEY_MODE, null)
+            ?.let { name -> ThemeMode.entries.firstOrNull { it.name == name } }
+            ?: defaults.mode
+        return ThemeSettings(mode = mode, dynamicColor = preferences.getBoolean(KEY_DYNAMIC_COLOR, defaults.dynamicColor))
+    }
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun save(settings: ThemeSettings) {
+        commit {
+            putString(KEY_MODE, settings.mode.name)
+            putBoolean(KEY_DYNAMIC_COLOR, settings.dynamicColor)
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Goes back to the defaults. */
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun clear() {
+        commit { clear() }
+    }
+
+    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
+        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
+    }
+
+    internal companion object {
+        const val FILE_NAME = "theme_settings"
+        private const val KEY_MODE = "mode"
+        private const val KEY_DYNAMIC_COLOR = "dynamic_color"
+    }
+}

+/** How a sign-in attempt ended. None of these carries a credential. */
+sealed interface SignInResult {
+
+    /** The server and the token are stored. */
+    data object SignedIn : SignInResult
+
[credential-related line omitted]
+    data object BadCredentials : SignInResult
+
+    /** 429: too many failed attempts. [retryAfterSeconds] is null without a usable `Retry-After`. */
+    data class RateLimited(val retryAfterSeconds: Long?) : SignInResult
+
+    /**
+     * The TLS handshake failed because no system or user CA trusts the
+     * server's certificate. No request was sent, so no credential left the
+     * phone. TKA-0021 turns this into the prompt to trust a self-signed
+     * certificate by its fingerprint.
+     */
+    data object UntrustedCertificate : SignInResult
+
+    /**
+     * The server redirected to [origin] (`scheme://host:port`), and the app
+     * talks only to the server the user entered.
+     */
+    data class HostNotAllowed(val origin: String) : SignInResult
+
+    /** No response: wrong host, no route, refused connection, other TLS failure or timeout. */
+    data object Unreachable : SignInResult
+
+    /** Any other status outside 2xx, such as 404 for a URL that is not a Tokkl server. */
+    data class UnexpectedStatus(val status: Int) : SignInResult
+
+    /** A 2xx answer that is not a `GET /api/v9/me` user. */
+    data object NotTokkl : SignInResult
+
[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    /** The server accepted the credentials, but the phone could not store the token. */
+    data object StorageFailed : SignInResult
+}
+
+/** Stores the configured server and its API token once the server has accepted them. */
+fun interface SessionWriter {
+    @Throws(GeneralSecurityException::class, IOException::class)
+    fun save(server: ServerUrl, token: String)
+}

+/**
+ * One sign-in attempt: `GET /api/v9/me` with HTTP Basic auth, then the server
[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+ * and never written anywhere.
+ *
+ * The call blocks, so make it off the main thread.
+ */
+class SignIn(
+    private val session: SessionWriter,
+    private val httpClient: OkHttpClient = SharedHttpClient.instance,
+) {
+
+    fun attempt(server: ServerUrl, credentials: SignInCredentials): SignInResult {
+        val client = TogglApiClient(server, credentials.toApiCredentials(), httpClient)
+        // Until the server is stored, the URL being tried is the configured server.
+        val result = ConfiguredServer.signingIn(server) { client.get(server.api("me"), Me.serializer()) }
+        val me = when (result) {
+            is ApiResult.Success -> result.value
+            is ApiResult.Failure -> return result.error.toSignInResult()
+        }

[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+        }

+        } catch (e: GeneralSecurityException) {
+            SignInResult.StorageFailed
+        } catch (e: IOException) {
+            SignInResult.StorageFailed
+        }
+    }

[credential-related line omitted]
[credential-related line omitted]
+    }
+
+private fun ApiError.toSignInResult(): SignInResult =
+    when (this) {
+        ApiError.AuthFailed -> SignInResult.BadCredentials
+        is ApiError.RateLimited -> SignInResult.RateLimited(retryAfterSeconds)
+        is ApiError.UnexpectedStatus -> SignInResult.UnexpectedStatus(status)
+        is ApiError.InvalidResponse -> SignInResult.NotTokkl
+        is ApiError.HostNotAllowed -> SignInResult.HostNotAllowed(origin)
+        is ApiError.Network -> if (cause.isUntrustedCertificate()) SignInResult.UntrustedCertificate else SignInResult.Unreachable
+    }
+
+/**
+ * Android reports a chain no trusted CA signed as an [SSLHandshakeException]
+ * caused by a [CertificateException] or [CertPathValidatorException]
+ * ("Trust anchor for certification path not found").
+ */
+internal fun IOException.isUntrustedCertificate(): Boolean =
+    this is SSLHandshakeException &&
+        generateSequence(cause) { it.cause }.take(MAX_CAUSES).any { it is CertificateException || it is CertPathValidatorException }
+
+/** Enough for Android's TLS errors, and an end to a cycle of causes. */

+/**
+ * The sign-in form and its attempts. Each submit sends one request, and only

+        val left = retryAt - clock()
+        return if (left <= 0) 0 else (left + MILLIS_PER_SECOND - 1) / MILLIS_PER_SECOND
+    }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import java.time.Instant
+import java.time.ZoneId
+import java.time.temporal.ChronoUnit
+import java.time.zone.ZoneRules
+import kotlin.math.abs
+import kotlin.math.ceil
+import kotlin.math.floor
+
+// Port of isTimeResolution and snapSpan in ../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts at tokkl commit
+// 51aed28fbc2769e4868f915230a558c2f2ce989e, with timeZoneOffsetMs from time.ts next to it.
+// SnapTest replays the cases from snap.test.ts.
+//
+// As in JavaScript, the arithmetic runs on doubles that hold epoch milliseconds, and an instant
+// outside the range of a JavaScript Date behaves like an invalid Date.
+
+private const val MINUTE_MS = 60_000.0
+
+// A JavaScript Date holds up to 8.64e15 ms either side of the epoch.
+private const val MAX_TIME_MS = 8_640_000_000_000_000L
+
+// The instants that fall within that range once cut to whole milliseconds.
+private val JS_DATE_RANGE =
+    Instant.ofEpochMilli(-MAX_TIME_MS)..<Instant.ofEpochMilli(MAX_TIME_MS + 1)
+
+data class SnappedSpan(val start: Instant, val stop: Instant)
+
+/**
+ * True for the resolutions that divide an hour evenly: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30 and
+ * 60.
+ */
+fun isTimeResolution(minutes: Double): Boolean =
+    minutes.isFinite() && floor(minutes) == minutes && minutes >= 1 && 60 % minutes == 0.0
+
+/**
+ * Snaps the span from [start] to [stop] to a resolution of [minutes], which must divide 60.
+ *
+ * The duration rounds up to a multiple of the resolution: it never shrinks, it gains less than one
+ * step, and a duration that is already a multiple stays as it is. The start moves to the nearest
+ * point of the grid on the wall clock of [timeZone], rounding a tie up. Because the resolution
+ * divides an hour, the grid falls on every whole hour, also in zones 30 or 45 minutes off UTC.
+ * Near a DST change each grid point uses the offset in force at that point, so a change of half an
+ * hour, as on Lord Howe Island, never leaves the start off the clock grid.
+ * The stop is the snapped start plus the rounded duration, counted in elapsed time, so a span
+ * across a DST change keeps its duration.
+ *
+ * [start] and [stop] are cut to whole milliseconds, the precision of a JavaScript Date. Where the
+ * TypeScript throws a RangeError, this throws [IllegalArgumentException]: for a resolution that
+ * does not divide 60, a stop before the start, an instant outside a JavaScript Date's range, and a
+ * start within a step or two of either end of that range. Within hours of the range's end the
+ * TypeScript can also return an invalid Date; an [Instant] has no such value, so this throws there
+ * too.
+ */
+fun snapSpan(start: Instant, stop: Instant, minutes: Double, timeZone: ZoneId): SnappedSpan {
+    require(isTimeResolution(minutes)) { "Invalid time resolution: $minutes" }
+    val startMs = jsTime(start)
+    val elapsed = jsTime(stop) - startMs
+    // Written like the TypeScript's !(elapsed >= 0), so NaN from an invalid Date fails it too.

CHUNK 23 OF 36
+    require(elapsed >= 0) { "Stop must not be before start" }
+    val step = minutes * MINUTE_MS
+    val duration = ceil(elapsed / step) * step
+    val snappedStart = nearestGridPoint(startMs, step, timeZone.rules)
+    return SnappedSpan(jsDate(snappedStart), jsDate(snappedStart + duration))
+}
+
+/**
+ * The span the server will store for a stopped entry it has not received yet, so the phone can
+ * show those times before sending it. Never pass the running entry: its clock is not snapped.
+ *
+ * A server without Tokkl's `time_resolution_minutes` in `GET /api/v9/me` does not snap, so with
+ * [timeResolutionMinutes] null the span comes back unchanged (docs/PRD.md section 8.3). A value
+ * that is not a resolution leaves it unchanged as well, where [snapSpan] would throw. Otherwise
+ * both ends are cut to whole seconds first, as Tokkl's server does before it snaps
+ * (snapToResolution in ../tokkl/packages/api/src/models/timeEntries.ts).
+ */
+fun snapUnsent(
+    start: Instant,
+    stop: Instant,
+    timeResolutionMinutes: Double?,
+    timeZone: ZoneId,
+): SnappedSpan {
+    if (timeResolutionMinutes == null || !isTimeResolution(timeResolutionMinutes)) {
+        return SnappedSpan(start, stop)
+    }
+    return snapSpan(
+        start.truncatedTo(ChronoUnit.SECONDS),
+        stop.truncatedTo(ChronoUnit.SECONDS),
+        timeResolutionMinutes,
+        timeZone,
+    )
+}
+
+/**
+ * The last instant at or before `time` whose wall-clock time is a multiple of `step` at `offset`.
+ */
+private fun gridFloor(time: Double, step: Double, offset: Double): Double =
+    floor((time + offset) / step) * step - offset
+
+/**
+ * The instant nearest `time` whose wall-clock time in `rules` is a multiple of `step`, taking
+ * the later one on a tie. Without an offset change within a step of `time`, the grid points on
+ * either side share the offset at `time`. Otherwise the candidates come from the offsets on both
+ * sides of the change, and each one counts only if it is on the grid with the offset in force at
+ * that instant, which also drops wall-clock times that a DST gap skips.
+ */
+private fun nearestGridPoint(time: Double, step: Double, rules: ZoneRules): Double {
+    val offsetAt = { at: Double -> offsetMs(at, rules) }
+    val offsets = listOf(time - step, time, time + step).map(offsetAt).distinct()
+    if (offsets.size == 1) {
+        val below = gridFloor(time, step, offsets[0])
+        return if ((time - below) * 2 >= step) below + step else below
+    }
+    return offsets
+        .flatMap { offset ->
+            val below = gridFloor(time, step, offset)
+            listOf(below - step, below, below + step, below + 2 * step)
+        }
+        .filter { candidate -> gridFloor(candidate, step, offsetAt(candidate)) == candidate }
+        .reduce { best, candidate ->
+            val nearer = abs(candidate - time) - abs(best - time)
+            if (nearer < 0 || (nearer == 0.0 && candidate > best)) candidate else best
+        }
+}
+
+/**
+ * The offset from UTC in force at `at`, in milliseconds, like timeZoneOffsetMs in time.ts. That
+ * one formats `new Date(at)` with Intl, which throws a RangeError for an invalid Date, and reads
+ * the wall clock back with Date.UTC, which gives NaN for a wall clock past the range's end.
+ */
+private fun offsetMs(at: Double, rules: ZoneRules): Double {
+    require(abs(at) <= MAX_TIME_MS) { "Invalid time value" }
+    val offset = rules.getOffset(Instant.ofEpochMilli(at.toLong())).totalSeconds * 1000.0
+    return if (floor(at / 1000) * 1000 + offset > MAX_TIME_MS) Double.NaN else offset
+}
+
+/** `instant.getTime()` of the JavaScript Date for [instant]: NaN outside a Date's range. */
+private fun jsTime(instant: Instant): Double =
+    if (instant in JS_DATE_RANGE) instant.toEpochMilli().toDouble() else Double.NaN
+
+/** The instant of `new Date(ms)`, refusing what would be an invalid Date. */
+private fun jsDate(ms: Double): Instant {
+    require(abs(ms) <= MAX_TIME_MS) { "Invalid time value" }
+    return Instant.ofEpochMilli(ms.toLong())
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import java.time.DateTimeException
+import java.time.ZoneId
+import java.time.ZoneOffset
+
+// Tokkl stores any timezone name that Intl.DateTimeFormat accepts (isValidTimeZone in
+// ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts) and returns it from GET /api/v9/me as sent. parseTimeZone
+// accepts the same names. The rules here were checked against Node 24.21 (ICU 78.3, tz 2026c).
+
+// Intl's offset syntax: an ASCII or U+2212 minus sign, hours 00 to 23, then optional minutes,
+// with or without a colon.
+private val OFFSET = Regex("""([+\-\u2212])([01][0-9]|2[0-3])(?::?([0-5][0-9]))?""")
+
+private const val MAX_OFFSET_SECONDS = 18 * 3600
+
+// The names ICU accepts that java.time does not list: the three-letter IDs it keeps from old Java,
+// and links tzdb has dropped. Each maps to the zone Intl resolves it to, which for EST, MST and
+// HST is a region rather than the fixed offset of ZoneId.SHORT_IDS.
+private val INTL_ONLY_NAMES = mapOf(
+    "ACT" to "Australia/Darwin",
+    "AET" to "Australia/Sydney",

+    "ART" to "Africa/Cairo",

+    "EAT" to "Africa/Nairobi",
+    "ECT" to "Europe/Paris",
+    "EST" to "America/Panama",
+    "GMT+0" to "Etc/GMT+0",
+    "GMT-0" to "Etc/GMT-0",
+    "HST" to "Pacific/Honolulu",
+    "IET" to "America/Indianapolis",
+    "IST" to "Asia/Calcutta",
+    "JST" to "Asia/Tokyo",
+    "MIT" to "Pacific/Apia",
+    "MST" to "America/Phoenix",
+    "NET" to "Asia/Yerevan",
+    "NST" to "Pacific/Auckland",
+    "PLT" to "Asia/Karachi",
+    "PNT" to "America/Phoenix",
+    "PRT" to "America/Puerto_Rico",
+    "PST" to "America/Los_Angeles",
+    "ROC" to "Asia/Taipei",
+    "SST" to "Pacific/Guadalcanal",
+    "US/Pacific-New" to "America/Los_Angeles",
+    "VST" to "Asia/Saigon",
+)

+// runtime's own list, so those names resolve the same on every tz database. Sorting first keeps
+// the result fixed should two IDs ever differ only in case.
+private val ZONE_IDS: Map<String, String> by lazy {

+        for ((name, id) in INTL_ONLY_NAMES) put(name.asciiLowercase(), id)
+        for (id in ZoneId.getAvailableZoneIds().sorted()) putIfAbsent(id.asciiLowercase(), id)
+    }
+}
+
+/**
+ * The zone for a timezone name from `GET /api/v9/me`, or null when there is none. Never falls back
+ * to the device's zone.
+ *
+ * Accepts what Intl accepts, which is more than [ZoneId.of]: names in any ASCII case (`utc`,
+ * `europe/madrid`), ICU's three-letter IDs such as `IST`, and offsets such as `+05`, `+0530` or
+ * `-05:00`, also with a U+2212 minus sign. Returns null for what Intl rejects, even where
+ * [ZoneId.of] accepts it, such as `Z`, `UT`, `GMT+5` or `UTC+01:00`.
+ *
+ * Two cases depend on the runtime. A name resolves only if the device's tz database lists it or
+ * INTL_ONLY_NAMES maps it. Offsets beyond ±18:00 return null, because [ZoneOffset] stops there
+ * while Intl goes to ±23:59.
+ */
+fun parseTimeZone(name: String): ZoneId? {
+    val offset = OFFSET.matchEntire(name)
+    if (offset != null) return parseOffset(offset)
+    val id = ZONE_IDS[name.asciiLowercase()] ?: return null
+    return try {
+        ZoneId.of(id)
+    } catch (e: DateTimeException) {
+        // A tz database without one of the targets in INTL_ONLY_NAMES.
+        null
+    }
+}
+
+private fun parseOffset(match: MatchResult): ZoneOffset? {
+    val sign = if (match.groupValues[1] == "+") 1 else -1
+    val hours = match.groupValues[2].toInt()
+    val minutes = match.groups[3]?.value?.toInt() ?: 0
+    val seconds = hours * 3600 + minutes * 60
+    if (seconds > MAX_OFFSET_SECONDS) return null
+    return ZoneOffset.ofTotalSeconds(sign * seconds)
+}
+
+/** Lowercases A to Z only, the case folding Intl uses for timezone names. */
+private fun String.asciiLowercase(): String =
+    buildString(length) { for (c in this@asciiLowercase) append(if (c in 'A'..'Z') c + 32 else c) }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import java.io.ByteArrayInputStream
+import java.io.DataInputStream
+import java.io.File
+import java.util.zip.ZipFile
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.Test
+
+/**
+ * Only [SharedHttpClient] may build an HTTP client, so the host guard and the
+ * other security settings cover every request. The test reads the constant
+ * pool of every compiled app class, so an import alias or a fully qualified
+ * name cannot hide a use.
+ */
+class SingleHttpClientTest {
+
+    private val classes: Map<String, Set<String>> by lazy { appClassReferences() }
+
+    @Test
+    fun noAppClassButSharedHttpClientBuildsAnHttpClient() {
+        assertTrue("the scan found the app's classes", "com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity" in classes)
+
+        val offenders = classes
+            .filterKeys { !isSharedHttpClient(it) }
+            .flatMap { (name, references) -> references.filter(::isForbidden).map { "$name uses $it" } }
+
+        assertTrue("Use SharedHttpClient.instance instead:\n" + offenders.joinToString("\n"), offenders.isEmpty())
+    }
+
+    /** Shows the scan sees a client being built, so the test above cannot pass by finding nothing. */
+    @Test
+    fun scanSeesTheSharedClientBeingBuilt() {
+        val shared = classes.filterKeys(::isSharedHttpClient).values.flatten()
+
+        assertTrue(shared.any { it == "okhttp3/OkHttpClient\$Builder.<init>" && isForbidden(it) })
+    }
+
+    private fun isSharedHttpClient(className: String): Boolean =
+        className == SHARED || className.startsWith("$SHARED\$")
+
+    private fun isForbidden(reference: String): Boolean =
+        FORBIDDEN.any { prefix ->
+            reference == prefix ||
+                (prefix.endsWith("/") && reference.startsWith(prefix)) ||
+                reference.startsWith("$prefix.")
+        }
+
+    /** Class name to the classes and members its constant pool refers to, as `owner` or `owner.member`. */
+    private fun appClassReferences(): Map<String, Set<String>> {
+        val location = File(SharedHttpClient::class.java.protectionDomain!!.codeSource.location.toURI())
+        val classFiles: Map<String, ByteArray> = if (location.isDirectory) {
+            location.walkTopDown()
+                .filter { it.isFile && it.name.endsWith(".class") }
+                .associate { it.relativeTo(location).invariantSeparatorsPath.removeSuffix(".class") to it.readBytes() }
+        } else {
+            ZipFile(location).use { jar ->
+                jar.entries().asSequence()
+                    .filter { it.name.endsWith(".class") }
+                    .associate { it.name.removeSuffix(".class") to jar.getInputStream(it).use { s -> s.readBytes() } }
+            }
+        }
+        return classFiles
+            .filterKeys { it.startsWith(APP_PACKAGE) }
+            .mapValues { (_, bytes) -> references(bytes) }
+    }
+
+    /** Reads the class and member references from a class file's constant pool (JVMS 4.4). */
+    private fun references(bytes: ByteArray): Set<String> {
+        val input = DataInputStream(ByteArrayInputStream(bytes))
+        check(input.readInt() == CLASS_MAGIC) { "not a class file" }
+        input.readUnsignedShort()
+        input.readUnsignedShort()
+        val count = input.readUnsignedShort()
+        val utf8 = arrayOfNulls<String>(count)
+        val classNameIndex = IntArray(count)
+        val nameAndTypeNameIndex = IntArray(count)
+        val classEntries = mutableListOf<Int>()
+        val memberEntries = mutableListOf<Pair<Int, Int>>()
+        var index = 1
+        while (index < count) {
+            when (val tag = input.readUnsignedByte()) {
+                TAG_UTF8 -> utf8[index] = input.readUTF()
+                TAG_INTEGER, TAG_FLOAT -> input.readInt()
+                TAG_LONG, TAG_DOUBLE -> {
+                    input.readLong()
+                    index++ // Takes two entries.
+                }
+                TAG_CLASS -> {
+                    classNameIndex[index] = input.readUnsignedShort()
+                    classEntries += index
+                }
+                TAG_STRING, TAG_METHOD_TYPE, TAG_MODULE, TAG_PACKAGE -> input.readUnsignedShort()
+                TAG_FIELD_REF, TAG_METHOD_REF, TAG_INTERFACE_METHOD_REF ->
+                    memberEntries += input.readUnsignedShort() to input.readUnsignedShort()
+                TAG_NAME_AND_TYPE -> {
+                    nameAndTypeNameIndex[index] = input.readUnsignedShort()
+                    input.readUnsignedShort()
+                }
+                TAG_METHOD_HANDLE -> {
+                    input.readUnsignedByte()
+                    input.readUnsignedShort()
+                }
+                TAG_DYNAMIC, TAG_INVOKE_DYNAMIC -> {
+                    input.readUnsignedShort()
+                    input.readUnsignedShort()
+                }
+                else -> error("unknown constant pool tag $tag")
+            }
+            index++
+        }
+        val classNames = classEntries.map { utf8[classNameIndex[it]]!! }
+        val members = memberEntries.map { (owner, nameAndType) ->


exec

+        }
+        return (classNames + members).toSet()
+    }
+
+    private companion object {
+        const val APP_PACKAGE = "com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/"
+        const val SHARED = "com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/SharedHttpClient"
+
+        /** Ways to build an HTTP client or open a connection without the shared client. */
+        val FORBIDDEN = listOf(

+            "okhttp3/OkHttpClient.<init>",
+            "okhttp3/OkHttpClient.newBuilder",

+            "javax/net/ssl/SSLSocketFactory.createSocket",
+            "android/webkit/WebView",
+            "android/app/DownloadManager",
+        )
+
+        const val CLASS_MAGIC = 0xCAFEBABE.toInt()
+        const val TAG_UTF8 = 1
+        const val TAG_INTEGER = 3
+        const val TAG_FLOAT = 4
+        const val TAG_LONG = 5
+        const val TAG_DOUBLE = 6
+        const val TAG_CLASS = 7
+        const val TAG_STRING = 8
+        const val TAG_FIELD_REF = 9
+        const val TAG_METHOD_REF = 10
+        const val TAG_INTERFACE_METHOD_REF = 11
+        const val TAG_NAME_AND_TYPE = 12
+        const val TAG_METHOD_HANDLE = 15
+        const val TAG_METHOD_TYPE = 16
+        const val TAG_DYNAMIC = 17
+        const val TAG_INVOKE_DYNAMIC = 18
+        const val TAG_MODULE = 19
+        const val TAG_PACKAGE = 20
+    }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.settings
+
+import java.io.IOException
+import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
+import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Test
+
+class ThemeSettingsModelTest {
+
+    private val saved = mutableListOf<ThemeSettings>()
+    private var failSaves = false
+
+    private fun model(initial: ThemeSettings = ThemeSettings()) =
+        ThemeSettingsModel(
+            initial = initial,
+            save = { if (failSaves) throw IOException("disk full") else saved += it },
+            scope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.Unconfined),
+            io = Dispatchers.Unconfined,
+        )
+
+    @Test
+    fun startsWithTheStoredSettings() {
+        val stored = ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.DARK, dynamicColor = true)
+
+        assertEquals(stored, model(stored).state.value)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun eachChangeShowsAndIsSaved() {
+        val model = model()
+
+        model.onModeChange(ThemeMode.DARK)
+        assertEquals(ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.DARK), model.state.value)
+        model.onDynamicColorChange(true)
+        assertEquals(ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.DARK, dynamicColor = true), model.state.value)
+        model.onModeChange(ThemeMode.SYSTEM)
+
+        assertEquals(
+            listOf(
+                ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.DARK),
+                ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.DARK, dynamicColor = true),
+                ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.SYSTEM, dynamicColor = true),
+            ),
+            saved,
+        )
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun choosingWhatIsStoredWritesNothing() {
+        val model = model(ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.LIGHT))
+
+        model.onModeChange(ThemeMode.LIGHT)
+
+        assertEquals(emptyList<ThemeSettings>(), saved)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun aFailedWriteGoesBackToTheStoredSettings() {
+        val model = model(ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.LIGHT))
+        failSaves = true
+
+        model.onModeChange(ThemeMode.DARK)
+
+        assertEquals(ThemeSettings(ThemeMode.LIGHT), model.state.value)
+    }
+}

+        for (sdkInt in 26..30) assertFalse("API $sdkInt", dynamicColorAvailable(sdkInt))
+        for (sdkInt in 31..37) assertTrue("API $sdkInt", dynamicColorAvailable(sdkInt))

+// Cases copied verbatim from ../tokkl/packages/common/snap.test.ts at tokkl commit
+// 51aed28fbc2769e4868f915230a558c2f2ce989e, for the port of isTimeResolution and snapSpan in
+// ../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts at the same commit.
+// Diff the TypeScript file against this one to spot drift.
+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertThrows
+import org.junit.Test
+import java.time.Instant
+import java.time.ZoneId
+import java.time.ZoneOffset
+import java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter
+
+class SnapTest {
+    /** JavaScript's `Date.prototype.toISOString`, which always shows milliseconds. */
+    private val isoString = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("uuuu-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss.SSS'Z'").withZone(ZoneOffset.UTC)
+
+    /** Snaps an ISO span and returns the result as ISO strings. */
+    private fun snap(start: String, stop: String, minutes: Int, timeZone: String = "UTC"): List<String> {
+        val span = snapSpan(Instant.parse(start), Instant.parse(stop), minutes.toDouble(), ZoneId.of(timeZone))
+        return listOf(isoString.format(span.start), isoString.format(span.stop))
+    }
+
+    private fun minutesBetween(span: List<String>): Double =
+        (Instant.parse(span[1]).toEpochMilli() - Instant.parse(span[0]).toEpochMilli()) / 60_000.0
+
+    @Test
+    fun `a duration that is already a multiple of the resolution keeps its length`() {
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:45:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:45:00Z", 15))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", 15))
+        // Off the grid, the start moves but the duration stays 30 minutes.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:30:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:02:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:32:00Z", 15))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `the duration rounds up, padding less than one step`() {
+        assertEquals(15.0, minutesBetween(snap("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:00:01Z", 15)), 0.0)
+        assertEquals(30.0, minutesBetween(snap("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:15:01Z", 15)), 0.0)
+        assertEquals(30.0, minutesBetween(snap("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:29:59Z", 15)), 0.0)
+        assertEquals(5.0, minutesBetween(snap("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z", "2026-10-14T09:04:59.999Z", 5)), 0.0)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `the start snaps to the nearest grid point, down or up, and a tie goes up`() {
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:15:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:07:29Z", "2026-10-14T09:20:00Z", 15))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:15:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:30:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:07:31Z", "2026-10-14T09:20:00Z", 15))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:15:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:30:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:07:30Z", "2026-10-14T09:20:00Z", 15))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `a resolution of one minute snaps to whole minutes and one of 60 to whole hours`() {
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:10:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:00:29Z", "2026-10-14T09:10:00Z", 1))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:01:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T09:11:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:00:30Z", "2026-10-14T09:10:10Z", 1))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T09:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T11:00:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T09:20:00Z", "2026-10-14T10:25:00Z", 60))

+    @Test
+    fun `the grid follows the account clock in zones 30 or 45 minutes off UTC`() {
+        // Asia/Kolkata is UTC+5:30, so its whole hours fall at half past in UTC.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T04:30:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T05:30:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T04:40:00Z", "2026-10-14T05:20:00Z", 60, "Asia/Kolkata"))
+        // Asia/Kathmandu is UTC+5:45: local 10:00 is 04:15 UTC and local 10:30 is 04:45 UTC.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T04:15:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T05:15:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T04:20:00Z", "2026-10-14T05:00:00Z", 60, "Asia/Kathmandu"))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T04:45:00.000Z", "2026-10-14T05:15:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T04:40:00Z", "2026-10-14T05:00:00Z", 30, "Asia/Kathmandu"))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `a span across midnight snaps like any other`() {
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-14T23:45:00.000Z", "2026-10-15T00:15:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T23:52:00Z", "2026-10-15T00:20:00Z", 15))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-15T00:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-15T00:30:00.000Z"), snap("2026-10-14T23:53:00Z", "2026-10-15T00:20:00Z", 15))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `a span across a DST change keeps its rounded duration as elapsed time`() {
+        // Europe/Madrid falls back from 03:00 CEST to 02:00 CET at 01:00 UTC on 2026-10-25.
+        val autumn = snap("2026-10-25T00:20:00Z", "2026-10-25T01:50:00Z", 60, "Europe/Madrid")
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-25T00:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-25T02:00:00.000Z"), autumn)
+        assertEquals(120.0, minutesBetween(autumn), 0.0)
+        // Europe/Madrid springs forward from 02:00 CET to 03:00 CEST at 01:00 UTC on 2026-03-29.
+        val spring = snap("2026-03-29T00:50:00Z", "2026-03-29T01:40:00Z", 30, "Europe/Madrid")
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-03-29T01:00:00.000Z", "2026-03-29T02:00:00.000Z"), spring)
+        assertEquals(60.0, minutesBetween(spring), 0.0)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `the start stays on the clock grid across a DST change of half an hour`() {
+        val lordHowe = { start: String, stop: String, minutes: Int -> snap(start, stop, minutes, "Australia/Lord_Howe") }
+        // Lord Howe Island springs forward from 02:00 (UTC+10:30) to 02:30 (UTC+11) at 15:30 UTC on
+        // 2026-10-03. Local 01:00 is 14:30 UTC, 02:00 never happens and local 03:00 is 16:00 UTC.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-03T16:00:00.000Z", "2026-10-03T17:00:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-10-03T15:20:00Z", "2026-10-03T15:25:00Z", 60))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-03T14:30:00.000Z", "2026-10-03T15:30:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-10-03T15:10:00Z", "2026-10-03T15:25:00Z", 60))
+        // Local 02:30 exists on that day, and it is on the grid at a resolution of 30.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-10-03T15:30:00.000Z", "2026-10-03T16:00:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-10-03T15:20:00Z", "2026-10-03T15:25:00Z", 30))
+        // It falls back from 02:00 (UTC+11) to 01:30 (UTC+10:30) at 15:00 UTC on 2026-04-04. Local 01:00
+        // is 14:00 UTC and local 02:00 is 15:30 UTC; a tie between them goes to the later one.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-04-04T14:00:00.000Z", "2026-04-04T15:00:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-04-04T14:40:00Z", "2026-04-04T14:45:00Z", 60))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-04-04T15:30:00.000Z", "2026-04-04T16:30:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-04-04T15:10:00Z", "2026-04-04T15:15:00Z", 60))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-04-04T15:30:00.000Z", "2026-04-04T16:30:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-04-04T14:45:00Z", "2026-04-04T14:50:00Z", 60))
+        // Local 01:30 happens twice, at 14:30 and 15:00 UTC, and both are on the grid at 30.
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-04-04T15:00:00.000Z", "2026-04-04T15:30:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-04-04T14:50:00Z", "2026-04-04T14:55:00Z", 30))
+        assertEquals(listOf("2026-04-04T14:30:00.000Z", "2026-04-04T15:00:00.000Z"), lordHowe("2026-04-04T14:35:00Z", "2026-04-04T14:40:00Z", 30))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `only divisors of 60 are resolutions, and a stop before the start is refused`() {
+        for (minutes in listOf(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60)) assertEquals(minutes.toString(), true, isTimeResolution(minutes.toDouble()))
+        for (minutes in listOf(0.0, -5.0, 7.0, 45.0, 90.0, 120.0, 2.5, Double.NaN)) assertEquals(minutes.toString(), false, isTimeResolution(minutes))

+    }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertThrows
+import org.junit.Test
+import java.time.Instant
+import java.time.ZoneId
+
+class SnapUnsentTest {
+    private val madrid = ZoneId.of("Europe/Madrid")
+
+    // Off the grid at every resolution and not on a whole second, so any snapping or cutting shows.
+    private val start = Instant.parse("2026-10-14T09:02:03.456Z")
+    private val stop = Instant.parse("2026-10-14T09:31:00.789Z")
+
+    @Test
+    fun `without a time resolution the span comes back unchanged`() {
+        assertEquals(SnappedSpan(start, stop), snapUnsent(start, stop, null, madrid))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `a value that is not a time resolution leaves the span unchanged`() {
+        for (minutes in listOf(0.0, -5.0, 7.0, 45.0, 90.0, 2.5, Double.NaN, Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)) {
+            assertEquals(minutes.toString(), SnappedSpan(start, stop), snapUnsent(start, stop, minutes, madrid))
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `with a time resolution the span snaps like snapSpan`() {
+        assertEquals(snapSpan(start, stop, 15.0, madrid), snapUnsent(start, stop, 15.0, madrid))
+        assertEquals(
+            SnappedSpan(Instant.parse("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z"), Instant.parse("2026-10-14T09:30:00Z")),
+            snapUnsent(start, stop, 15.0, madrid),
+        )
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `both ends are cut to whole seconds before snapping, as the server does`() {
+        // 60.5 seconds round up to two minutes, but the server keeps 60 of them, which is one.
+        val from = Instant.parse("2026-10-14T09:00:00Z")

+// Pins for parseTimeZone. Whether each name is accepted, and the zone it maps to, was checked
+// against Intl.DateTimeFormat in Node 24.21 (ICU 78.3, tz 2026c), the check Tokkl's
+// isValidTimeZone runs, except where a case says the port departs on purpose.
+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
+import org.junit.Test
+import java.time.ZoneId
+import java.time.ZoneOffset
+
+class TimeZoneTest {
+    private fun assertResolves(cases: List<Pair<String, String>>) {
+        for ((name, id) in cases) assertEquals("name \"$name\"", ZoneId.of(id), parseTimeZone(name))
+    }
+
+    private fun assertRejects(names: List<String>) {
+        for (name in names) assertNull("name \"$name\"", parseTimeZone(name))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `canonical names resolve unchanged`() = assertResolves(
+        listOf(
+            "Europe/Madrid" to "Europe/Madrid",
+            "Australia/Lord_Howe" to "Australia/Lord_Howe",
+            "Asia/Kolkata" to "Asia/Kolkata",
+            "UTC" to "UTC",
+            "Etc/GMT+5" to "Etc/GMT+5",
+        ),
+    )
+
+    @Test
+    fun `names match in any ASCII case, as in Intl`() = assertResolves(
+        listOf(
+            "europe/madrid" to "Europe/Madrid",
+            "EUROPE/MADRID" to "Europe/Madrid",
+            "eUROPE/mADRID" to "Europe/Madrid",
+            "utc" to "UTC",
+            "etc/utc" to "Etc/UTC",
+            "gmt" to "GMT",
+            "us/pacific" to "US/Pacific",
+            "etc/gmt-14" to "Etc/GMT-14",
+            "asia/calcutta" to "Asia/Calcutta",
+        ),
+    )
+
+    @Test
+    fun `names only ICU knows map to the zone Intl resolves them to`() = assertResolves(
+        listOf(
+            "ACT" to "Australia/Darwin",
+            "AET" to "Australia/Sydney",
+            "AGT" to "America/Buenos_Aires",
+            "ART" to "Africa/Cairo",
+            "AST" to "America/Anchorage",
+            "BET" to "America/Sao_Paulo",
+            "BST" to "Asia/Dhaka",
+            "CAT" to "Africa/Maputo",
+            "CNT" to "America/St_Johns",
+            "CST" to "America/Chicago",
+            "CTT" to "Asia/Shanghai",
+            "Canada/East-Saskatchewan" to "America/Regina",
+            "EAT" to "Africa/Nairobi",
+            "ECT" to "Europe/Paris",
+            "EST" to "America/Panama",
+            // Intl calls these UTC.
+            "GMT+0" to "Etc/GMT+0",
+            "GMT-0" to "Etc/GMT-0",
+            "HST" to "Pacific/Honolulu",
+            "IET" to "America/Indianapolis",
+            "IST" to "Asia/Calcutta",

+            "-18:00" to ZoneOffset.ofHours(-18),
+        )

+    }

+        ),
+    )

+        // The port departs here on purpose: ZoneOffset stops at 18 hours either way.
+        assertRejects(listOf("+18:01", "+19:00", "+23:59", "-23:59"))
+    }

+# Reports why the emulator this script booted exited on its own, and forgets
+# it. The OOM killer ends qemu with SIGKILL, which wait reports as 137.
+# ShellCheck 0.11 misses that the EXIT trap runs cleanup, which calls this.
+# shellcheck disable=SC2329
+report_emulator_exit() {
+    local status=0
+    wait "$emulator_pid" 2>/dev/null || status=$?
+    emulator_pid=''
+    if [ "$status" -eq 137 ]; then
+        echo "$0: the emulator was killed with SIGKILL, most likely by the kernel's OOM killer." >&2
+        echo "$0: \`journalctl -k --grep=oom\` shows OOM kills. Free memory before running the tests again." >&2
+    else
+        echo "$0: the emulator exited with status $status. Last emulator output:" >&2
+        tail -n 20 "$work_dir/emulator.log" >&2
+    fi

+# AGP's test task can pass when a device dies between two tests. With the
+# emulator killed a second into the run, Gradle printed BUILD SUCCESSFUL and
+# had run none of the 32 tests. So every test device must still be online.
+gone=()
+for device in "${devices[@]}"; do
+    if [ "$(adb -s "$device" get-state 2>/dev/null)" != device ]; then gone+=("$device"); fi
+done
+if [ "${#gone[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
+    echo "$0: ${gone[*]} went away during the run. Gradle can pass without running every test, so this run fails." >&2
+    exit 1
+fi
+exit "$gradle_status"

+# Checks that the unsigned release APK is reproducible: builds it twice from
+# clean and compares the two APKs byte for byte.
+#
+#   scripts/reproducible-release.sh [REVISION]
+#
+# Each build runs in a new clone of REVISION (default HEAD), the way F-Droid
+# builds a release tag, so uncommitted changes are not built. The two clones
+# sit at paths of different lengths, so an embedded build path shows up as a
+# difference. The second build sees at most two CPU cores and gets its own
+# Gradle user home, which reads downloaded dependencies from yours but redoes
+# every transform, such as dexing the libraries. So output that depends on the
+# core count or on a cached transform shows up too. Both builds run without the
+# release signing variables, and with --no-daemon so they share no daemon.
+#
+# The clones and the second Gradle home live in build/reproducible-release.*
+# of this checkout, up to about 700 MB, and go away at the end.
+#
+# Exits 0 when the APKs match and 1 when they differ. On a difference it lists
+# the zip entries that differ and keeps both APKs for inspection.
+
+set -euo pipefail
+
+readonly APK='app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release-unsigned.apk'
+readonly SIGNING_VARIABLES=(
+    TOKKL_RELEASE_KEYSTORE
[credential-related line omitted]
+    TOKKL_RELEASE_KEY_ALIAS
[credential-related line omitted]
+)
+
+repo=$(git -C "$(dirname "$0")" rev-parse --show-toplevel)
+git_dir=$(git -C "$repo" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)
+revision=$(git -C "$repo" rev-parse --verify --quiet "${1:-HEAD}^{commit}") || {
+    echo "$0: ${1:-HEAD} is not a commit" >&2
+    exit 2
+}
+
+mkdir -p "$repo/build"
+work_dir=$(mktemp -d "$repo/build/reproducible-release.XXXXXX")
+trap 'rm -rf "$work_dir"' EXIT
+
+unset_signing=()
+for variable in "${SIGNING_VARIABLES[@]}"; do
+    unset_signing+=(-u "$variable")
+done
+
+# Builds the release APK of $revision in a new clone at $1 and copies it to $2.
+# The remaining arguments go in front of the Gradle command, through env.
+build() {
+    local tree=$1 out=$2
+    shift 2
+    git clone --quiet --no-checkout "$git_dir" "$tree"
+    git -C "$tree" -c advice.detachedHead=false checkout --quiet --detach "$revision"
+    # local.properties is ignored by git; it may hold sdk.dir.
+    if [ -f "$repo/local.properties" ]; then
+        cp "$repo/local.properties" "$tree/"
+    fi
+    echo "Building $(git -C "$tree" rev-parse --short HEAD) in $tree"
+    if ! (cd "$tree" && env "${unset_signing[@]}" "$@" ./gradlew --no-daemon assembleRelease) \
+        >"$tree.log" 2>&1; then
+        tail -n 40 "$tree.log" >&2
+        echo "$0: the build in $tree failed" >&2
+        exit 1
+    fi
+    cp "$tree/$APK" "$out"
+}
+
+build "$work_dir/a/tokkl-android" "$work_dir/a.apk"
+
+# The first build has downloaded the Gradle distribution and the dependencies.
+# The second Gradle home links the distribution and reads the dependencies
+# through Gradle's read-only cache, so it downloads nothing.
+gradle_home=${GRADLE_USER_HOME:-$HOME/.gradle}
+second_home="$work_dir/gradle-home"
+mkdir -p "$second_home/wrapper"
+ln -s "$gradle_home/wrapper/dists" "$second_home/wrapper/dists"
+second=(GRADLE_USER_HOME="$second_home" GRADLE_RO_DEP_CACHE="$gradle_home/caches")
+if command -v taskset >/dev/null && [ "$(nproc)" -gt 2 ]; then
+    second+=(taskset -c 0,1)
+fi
+build "$work_dir/second-build/tokkl-android" "$work_dir/b.apk" "${second[@]}"
+
+sha256sum "$work_dir/a.apk" "$work_dir/b.apk" | sed "s|$work_dir/||"
+if cmp -s "$work_dir/a.apk" "$work_dir/b.apk"; then
+    echo "The two unsigned release APKs of $revision are identical."
+    exit 0
+fi
+
+echo "The two unsigned release APKs of $revision differ." >&2
+# One line per entry: size, compressed size, CRC-32 and name, in zip order.
+entries() {
+    unzip -v "$1" | awk 'NR > 3 && $1 ~ /^[0-9]+$/ && NF >= 8 { print $1, $3, $7, $8 }'
+}
+if diff <(entries "$work_dir/a.apk") <(entries "$work_dir/b.apk") >&2; then
+    echo "Every entry has the same size and CRC-32; the difference is in the zip metadata." >&2
+fi
+keep=$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/reproducible-release.XXXXXX")
+cp "$work_dir/a.apk" "$work_dir/b.apk" "$keep/"
+echo "Both APKs are in $keep" >&2
+exit 1

# Machine context

- This machine is a slabtop: it has no built-in screen and no external monitor.
- Graphics are only needed for remote viewing of desktop apps (Electron) and for agents to screenshot them. Zero physical displays is expected.
- The graphical session is a headless sway compositor (`sway-headless.service`) shared by wayvnc (`wayvnc-headless.service`, VNC on the Tailscale address 100.127.76.70:5900 with no VNC login; ufw allows it only from punta-llarga, 100.91.141.70). Agents get `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-headless` and `DISPLAY=:0` from `t3.service`. Plasma, sddm and KRdp were removed from the boot path on 2026-10-02 (default target is `multi-user.target`). Setup and rollback are in `~/ai-env/headless-desktop.md`.
- When troubleshooting CPU or power use, preserve the two headless services and VNC access; prefer fixes that reduce idle work without disrupting remote access.
[credential-related line omitted]
- This machine is an always-on homelab server. Prioritize reliable unattended operation and idle energy efficiency while preserving required homelab services and VNC access.
- Keep Bluetooth and NFS/RPC enabled. KDE Connect and printing are not needed; the user requested KDE Connect removal on 2026-10-02.
- OS setting changes must survive reboots. Use durable configuration or enabled boot services, and verify persistence rather than relying only on runtime changes.
- Static DHCP is already configured for this server. Tailscale node-key expiry is disabled (user confirmed 2026-10-02).

- Use email for server alerts and maintenance reports.
[credential-related line omitted]

## Sending a reminder email

Agents can email the user a reminder (for example, a follow-up the user wants to deal with later) through the same encrypted pipeline:

```sh
{ echo 'kenkeep: stop tests leaking FOLDER_SUMMARIES into /tmp'; cat notes.md; } \
  | sudo -n /usr/local/libexec/slabtop-notify remind
```

- The first stdin line is the title and everything after it is the body. Titles are limited to 200 characters and the whole message to 64 KiB. The command accepts at most 10 reminders per hour, because reminders and server alerts share the SMTP2GO quota.
[credential-related line omitted]
- Never write a wrapper script and ask the user to run it with sudo. A file in the home directory can be changed by any process running as e0ipso, so running it as root hands root to that process. Pipe into the command above instead.
- The email subject is always `[punta-des-patro]: Server notification`. The title appears only inside the encrypted body, as `Reminder: <title>`.
- Installed and verified on 2026-10-02: a test reminder was delivered, and `sudo -n` refused other subcommands and extra arguments. The staging directory `~/.local/state/slabtop-remind` and the old `~/ai-env/reminders/send-reminder.sh` wrapper were removed.

## Text to speech (Kokoro)

Installed and verified on 2026-10-12. `kokoro-tts` is on `PATH` for every project and runs Kokoro-82M locally on the CPU, with no API key.

```sh
kokoro-tts script.txt "$tmp/out.mp3" --format mp3            # file in, mp3 out
echo 'Build finished.' | kokoro-tts - "$tmp/out.wav"          # stdin in
kokoro-tts script.txt "$tmp/out.wav" --voice bm_george --speed 1.1
kokoro-tts --help-voices                                      # 54 voices; af_heart is the default
```

- `~/.local/bin/kokoro-tts` is a wrapper around the upstream `kokoro-tts` CLI ([nazdridoy/kokoro-tts](https://github.com/nazdridoy/kokoro-tts), 2.3.2) in the venv `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/venv`. It adds `--model` and `--voices` pointing at the v1.0 files in `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/`, and `--voice af_heart` (or `$KOKORO_TTS_VOICE`), because the upstream default is an interactive voice picker that blocks an agent. Flags you pass explicitly win.
- Other flags: `--lang` (default `en-us`; see `--help-languages`), voice blends such as `--voice "af_sarah:60,am_adam:40"`, `.epub`/`.pdf` input, `--split-output <dir>`. Output is `wav` or `mp3`; use `ffmpeg` for anything else.
- Speed is roughly 170 words per minute at `--speed 1.0`. Rendering takes about a third of the audio's length on this machine, so a five minute clip takes under two minutes.
- Write scripts and audio to a temporary directory (`tmp=$(mktemp -d)`), never to the home directory, unless the user names a destination.
- To upgrade: `~/.local/share/kokoro-tts/venv/bin/python -m pip install -U kokoro-tts`. The venv has no system `ensurepip`; pip was bootstrapped into it.

You are an expert Git and GitHub operations specialist with deep expertise in version control workflows, repository management, and GitHub ecosystem tools. Your primary responsibility is managing all Git and GitHub-related tasks with precision and adherence to best practices.

**Core Responsibilities:**

- Execute all Git commands (commit, push, pull, branch, merge, rebase, stash, etc.)
- Manage GitHub operations using both web interface concepts and gh CLI
- Create properly formatted conventional commits by analyzing codebase patterns
- Handle pull request creation, management, and review processes
- Manage GitHub issues, labels, milestones, and project boards
- Monitor GitHub Actions workflows and manage artifacts
- Perform repository analysis, history exploration, and branch management
- Resolve merge conflicts and handle complex Git workflows

**Critical Requirements:**

- NEVER include any AI attribution in commits, pull requests, issues, or any repository metadata
- ALWAYS analyze existing commit history to understand and follow the project's conventional commit patterns
- Use conventional commit format (feat:, fix:, docs:, style:, refactor:, test:, chore:) based on project patterns
- Ensure all commit messages are clear, concise, and follow established project conventions
- **Follow the 50/72 rule:** subject line max 50 characters, body lines max 72 characters, blank line between subject and body
- Verify GitHub Actions status before and after operations when relevant
- **NEVER use `git add -A` or `git add .`** - Always stage files explicitly by specifying file paths
- **ALWAYS stage files explicitly:** `git add file1.md file2.md` rather than bulk staging
- **ASK the user if there are unstaged changes beyond what was requested** before proceeding with commits

**Conventional Commit Analysis Process:**

1. Examine recent commit history using `git log --oneline -20` to identify patterns
2. Analyze commit message structure, scope usage, and formatting conventions
3. Determine appropriate commit type based on changes made
4. Follow project-specific scope and format patterns
5. Create commit messages that maintain consistency with existing history

**Commit Message Formatting (50/72 Rule):**

1. **Subject line:** max 50 characters (including type prefix like `feat:`)
2. **Blank line:** mandatory separator between subject and body
3. **Body lines:** hard-wrap at 72 characters per line
4. If the subject cannot fit in 50 characters, move detail to the body
5. Validate line lengths before committing — reject and rewrite if over limits

**GitHub CLI Expertise:**

- Use `gh` commands for all GitHub operations when possible
- Handle authentication and repository context automatically
- Manage pull requests: create, review, merge, close
- Handle issues: create, assign, label, close
- Monitor workflows: check status, download artifacts, view logs
- Manage releases, tags, and repository settings

**Workflow Management:**

- Understand and implement GitFlow, GitHub Flow, and other branching strategies
- Handle complex scenarios: rebasing, cherry-picking, conflict resolution
- Manage remote repositories and upstream synchronization
- Coordinate between local and remote repository states

**Quality Assurance:**

- Always check repository status before major operations
- Verify branch state and clean working directory when needed
- Ensure proper remote tracking and upstream configuration
- Validate that operations complete successfully before proceeding
- Never disclose the use of AI in descriptions, messages, or anywhere else

**Communication Style:**

- Provide clear explanations of Git operations being performed
- Explain the reasoning behind commit message choices
- Offer alternative approaches when multiple valid options exist
- Warn about potentially destructive operations before executing

You should proactively suggest best practices and catch potential issues before they become problems. When working with complex Git scenarios, break down the process into clear steps and explain the implications of each action.

app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/TogglApiClient.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api

import java.io.IOException
import kotlinx.serialization.DeserializationStrategy
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonElement
import okhttp3.HttpUrl
import okhttp3.MediaType.Companion.toMediaType
import okhttp3.OkHttpClient
import okhttp3.Request
import okhttp3.RequestBody.Companion.toRequestBody
import okhttp3.Response

/**
 * Client for the Toggl Track API v9 and Reports API v3, as served by Tokkl or
 * by Toggl Track. Build endpoint URLs with [server], then call [get], [send]
 * or [sendNoContent]. Calls block the calling thread, so make them off the
 * main thread.
 */
class TogglApiClient(
    val server: ServerUrl,
    private val credentials: ApiCredentials,
    private val httpClient: OkHttpClient = SharedHttpClient.instance,
) {

    fun <T> get(url: HttpUrl, deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
        send("GET", url, body = null, deserializer)

    /**
     * Sends [body] as JSON, or no body when it is null, and decodes the 2xx
     * response body. An empty body does not decode, so use [sendNoContent]
     * for calls that answer without one. OkHttp refuses a null body for POST,
     * PUT and PATCH.
     */
    fun <T> send(
        method: String,
        url: HttpUrl,
        body: JsonElement?,
        deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>,
    ): ApiResult<T> = execute(method, url, body) { decode(it.body.string(), deserializer) }

    /**
     * Like [send], for calls whose response body the caller does not need,
     * such as Tokkl's DELETE of a time entry, task, client or tag, which
     * answer 200 with an empty body. Tokkl's project DELETE answers with the
     * project id as JSON, which this method ignores too. Any 2xx is a success.
     */
    fun sendNoContent(method: String, url: HttpUrl, body: JsonElement? = null): ApiResult<Unit> =
        execute(method, url, body) { ApiResult.Success(Unit) }

    private fun <T> execute(
        method: String,
        url: HttpUrl,
        body: JsonElement?,
        onSuccess: (Response) -> ApiResult<T>,
    ): ApiResult<T> {
        val request = Request.Builder()
            .url(url)
[credential-related line omitted]
            .method(method, body?.let { ApiJson.encodeToString(JsonElement.serializer(), it).toRequestBody(JSON) })
            .build()
        return try {
            httpClient.newCall(request).execute().use { response ->
                if (response.isSuccessful) onSuccess(response) else ApiResult.Failure(httpError(response))
            }
        } catch (e: HostNotAllowedException) {
            ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.HostNotAllowed(e.origin))
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.Network(e))
        }
    }

    private fun httpError(response: Response): ApiError.Http =
        when (response.code) {
            403 -> ApiError.AuthFailed
            429 -> ApiError.RateLimited(retryAfterSeconds(response.header("Retry-After")))
            else -> ApiError.UnexpectedStatus(response.code)
        }

    private fun <T> decode(text: String, deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
        try {
            ApiResult.Success(ApiJson.decodeFromString(deserializer, text))
        } catch (e: IllegalArgumentException) {
            // kotlinx.serialization's SerializationException is an IllegalArgumentException.
            ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.InvalidResponse(e))
        }

    private companion object {
        val JSON = "application/json; charset=utf-8".toMediaType()

        /** Tokkl sends whole seconds. An HTTP date or a negative number counts as missing. */
        fun retryAfterSeconds(value: String?): Long? = value?.trim()?.toLongOrNull()?.takeIf { it >= 0 }
    }
}

import android.os.Build

app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/LauncherIconTest.kt
package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl

import android.content.pm.LauncherApps
import android.graphics.drawable.AdaptiveIconDrawable
import android.graphics.drawable.BitmapDrawable
import android.graphics.drawable.ColorDrawable
import android.os.Build
import android.os.Process
import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNotNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test

/** The icon a launcher gets for the installed app, through the same API launchers use. */
class LauncherIconTest {

    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext

    @Test
    fun launcherGetsAnAdaptiveIconForMainActivity() {
        val entries = context.getSystemService(LauncherApps::class.java)
            .getActivityList(context.packageName, Process.myUserHandle())
        assertEquals(listOf(MainActivity::class.java.name), entries.map { it.componentName.className })

        val icon = entries.single().getIcon(0)

        assertTrue("the icon is a ${icon.javaClass.name}", icon is AdaptiveIconDrawable)
        icon as AdaptiveIconDrawable
        // The app's own layers, not a platform wrapper around a legacy icon.
        assertEquals(context.getColor(R.color.ic_launcher_background), (icon.background as? ColorDrawable)?.color)
        val artwork = (context.getDrawable(R.drawable.ic_launcher_foreground) as BitmapDrawable).bitmap
        val foreground = (icon.foreground as? BitmapDrawable)?.bitmap
        assertTrue("the foreground is not Tokkl's artwork", foreground?.sameAs(artwork) == true)
        if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.TIRAMISU) {
            assertNotNull("no monochrome layer for themed icons", icon.monochrome)
        }
    }
}

EXISTS ../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts True
/**
 * Snapping a time entry to a resolution: the step, in minutes, that every stored duration is a
 * multiple of. Shared by the server, which snaps on write, and the web app.
 */

import { timeZoneOffsetMs } from './time.ts';

const MINUTE_MS = 60_000;

export interface SnappedSpan {
  start: Date;
  stop: Date;
}

/** True for the resolutions that divide an hour evenly: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30 and 60. */
export function isTimeResolution(minutes: number): boolean {
  return Number.isInteger(minutes) && minutes >= 1 && 60 % minutes === 0;
}

/**
 * Snaps the span from `start` to `stop` to a resolution of `minutes`, which must divide 60.
 *
 * The duration rounds up to a multiple of the resolution: it never shrinks, it gains less than one
 * step, and a duration that is already a multiple stays as it is. The start moves to the nearest
 * point of the grid on the wall clock of `timeZone`, rounding a tie up. Because the resolution
 * divides an hour, the grid falls on every whole hour, also in zones 30 or 45 minutes off UTC.
 * Near a DST change each grid point uses the offset in force at that point, so a change of half an
 * hour, as on Lord Howe Island, never leaves the start off the clock grid.
 * The stop is the snapped start plus the rounded duration, counted in elapsed time, so a span
 * across a DST change keeps its duration.
 */
export function snapSpan(start: Date, stop: Date, minutes: number, timeZone: string): SnappedSpan {
  if (!isTimeResolution(minutes)) throw new RangeError(`Invalid time resolution: ${minutes}`);
  const elapsed = stop.getTime() - start.getTime();
  if (!(elapsed >= 0)) throw new RangeError('Stop must not be before start');
  const step = minutes * MINUTE_MS;
  const duration = Math.ceil(elapsed / step) * step;
  const snappedStart = nearestGridPoint(start.getTime(), step, timeZone);
  return { start: new Date(snappedStart), stop: new Date(snappedStart + duration) };
}

/** The last instant at or before `time` whose wall-clock time is a multiple of `step` at `offset`. */
function gridFloor(time: number, step: number, offset: number): number {
  return Math.floor((time + offset) / step) * step - offset;
}

/**
 * The instant nearest `time` whose wall-clock time in `timeZone` is a multiple of `step`, taking
 * the later one on a tie. Without an offset change within a step of `time`, the grid points on
 * either side share the offset at `time`. Otherwise the candidates come from the offsets on both
 * sides of the change, and each one counts only if it is on the grid with the offset in force at
 * that instant, which also drops wall-clock times that a DST gap skips.
 */
function nearestGridPoint(time: number, step: number, timeZone: string): number {
  const offsetAt = (at: number) => timeZoneOffsetMs(new Date(at), timeZone);
  const offsets = [...new Set([time - step, time, time + step].map(offsetAt))];
  if (offsets.length === 1) {
    const below = gridFloor(time, step, offsets[0]!);
    return (time - below) * 2 >= step ? below + step : below;
  }
  return offsets
    .flatMap((offset) => {
      const below = gridFloor(time, step, offset);
      return [below - step, below, below + step, below + 2 * step];
    })
    .filter((candidate) => gridFloor(candidate, step, offsetAt(candidate)) === candidate)
    .reduce((best, candidate) => {
      const nearer = Math.abs(candidate - time) - Math.abs(best - time);
      return nearer < 0 || (nearer === 0 && candidate > best) ? candidate : best;
    });
}

EXISTS ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts True
/**
 * Timezone helpers built on Intl. Instants are stored in UTC; calendar days
 * ("YYYY-MM-DD" keys) are always interpreted in the user's IANA timezone.
 */

import type { ZonedParts } from '@tokkl/types';

const formatters = new Map<string, Intl.DateTimeFormat>();
const WEEKDAYS = ['Sun', 'Mon', 'Tue', 'Wed', 'Thu', 'Fri', 'Sat'];

function formatterFor(timeZone: string): Intl.DateTimeFormat {
  let formatter = formatters.get(timeZone);
  if (!formatter) {
    formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', {
      timeZone,
      hourCycle: 'h23',
      year: 'numeric',
      month: '2-digit',
      day: '2-digit',
      hour: '2-digit',
      minute: '2-digit',
      second: '2-digit',
      weekday: 'short',
    });
    formatters.set(timeZone, formatter);
  }
  return formatter;
}

export function isValidTimeZone(timeZone: string): boolean {
  try {
    formatterFor(timeZone);
    return true;
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

export function zonedParts(date: Date, timeZone: string): ZonedParts {
  const parts: Record<string, string> = {};
  for (const part of formatterFor(timeZone).formatToParts(date)) parts[part.type] = part.value;
  return {
    year: Number(parts.year),
    month: Number(parts.month),
    day: Number(parts.day),
    hour: Number(parts.hour),
    minute: Number(parts.minute),
    second: Number(parts.second),
    weekday: WEEKDAYS.indexOf(parts.weekday!),
  };
}

/** Offset of `timeZone` from UTC at `date`, in milliseconds (positive east of UTC). */
export function timeZoneOffsetMs(date: Date, timeZone: string): number {
  const p = zonedParts(date, timeZone);
  const asUtc = Date.UTC(p.year, p.month - 1, p.day, p.hour, p.minute, p.second);
  return asUtc - Math.floor(date.getTime() / 1000) * 1000;
}

/** Converts a wall-clock time in `timeZone` to an instant. */
export function zonedToUtc(
  year: number, month: number, day: number,
  hour: number, minute: number, second: number,
  timeZone: string,
): Date {
  const wall = Date.UTC(year, month - 1, day, hour, minute, second);
  let guess = wall - timeZoneOffsetMs(new Date(wall), timeZone);
  // A second pass settles guesses that landed on the other side of a DST change.
  guess = wall - timeZoneOffsetMs(new Date(guess), timeZone);
  return new Date(guess);
}
